  • Chapter 5Identity, Secrets, and Audit Trust
  • Chapter 5ADeploying Microsoft Entra ID & Purview
  • Chapter 6Network and Observability
  • Chapter 6ADeploying Microsoft Fabric
  • Chapter 6BDeploying Sentinel & Defender
Chapter 4

Anatomy of the Platform Layer

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Enumerates every Microsoft Azure resource that constitutes Layer 1, with concrete resource types and API versions.
  • Maps each resource to its Sprint 1 Bicep module, its required SKU, and its tenant-level parameter inputs.
  • Establishes the Layer 1 outputs that every Layer 2 (Service) Bicep consumes.
60-second read
  • Layer 1 is exactly seven Microsoft Azure resource types, plus role assignments — every one named by Microsoft resource type and API version.
  • Premium SKUs are mandatory for Microsoft.ContainerRegistry and Microsoft.KeyVault (geo-rep · content trust · CMK · HSM are Premium-only).
  • Four user-assigned managed identities split runtime · MCP · ingest · pii-unlock blast radii — enforced at the Microsoft Entra ID layer.
  • Layer 1 outputs are the contract Layer 2 consumes; do not duplicate Platform resources inside a Service Bicep.

4.1 The Layer 1 Inventory, by Microsoft Resource Type

The APEX Platform layer is exactly seven Microsoft Azure resource types, deployed once per client tenant. The list below is canonical; the Sprint 1 Bicep in MVP/agentic-merch/bicep/modules/ is the implementation.

#Microsoft resource typeAPI versionSKU / tierBicep module
1Microsoft.ContainerRegistry/registries2023-11-01-previewPremium · zone-redundant · geo-replicatedacr.bicep
2Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities2023-01-31n/a (Microsoft Entra ID)identity.bicep
3Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults2023-07-01Premium (HSM-backed) · soft-delete + purge protectionkeyvault.bicep
4Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/keys2023-07-01RSA-HSM · 2048 · CMKkeyvault.bicep
5Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces2023-09-01PerGB2018 · 30-day retention (DEV) / 90-day (PROD)observability.bicep
6Microsoft.Insights/components2020-02-02Workspace-basedobservability.bicep
7Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments2024-03-01Consumption + Dedicated workload profiles · KEDA-enabledagents.bicep

Two role assignments and one Microsoft Entra ID role activation complete the layer:

#Microsoft resource typeAPI versionPurpose
8Microsoft.Authorization/roleAssignments2022-04-01AcrPull on registry to runtime managed identity
9Microsoft.Authorization/roleAssignments2022-04-01Key Vault Crypto User on Key Vault to runtime + MCP managed identities
10Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/accessPolicies2023-07-01Wrap/unwrap on CMK for runtime identity (legacy access-policy fallback for tenants not yet on RBAC mode)
Note on API versions

The API versions above are pinned to the versions used by the MVP Sprint 1 Bicep at the time of writing (May 2026). They will drift. The discipline is not "always use these versions"; the discipline is "every @version in your Bicep is an explicit, reviewed choice." When Microsoft promotes a preview API to GA, schedule a Layer 1 minor-version bump and re-deploy in DEV before promoting.

4.2 Microsoft.ContainerRegistry — The Container Registry

One Microsoft.ContainerRegistry/registries resource per tenant, Premium SKU. Premium is mandatory; the Layer 1 design depends on Premium-only features:

  • Geo-replication — image pulls from any client region without cross-region latency.
  • Content trust (Notation, Microsoft's signing client) — required for the cosign-verified mirror from the Deloitte authoring registry. The Microsoft Notation specification is the trust path; cosign signatures co-exist for cross-tool compatibility.
  • Zone redundancy — Premium-only, required for STAGE and PROD.
  • Customer-managed key encryption — the Premium Azure Container Registry encrypts at rest with the Layer 1 CMK from Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/keys, completing the BYOK chain.
BICEP — acr.bicep (excerpt)
resource acr 'Microsoft.ContainerRegistry/registries@2023-11-01-preview' = {
  name: 'apexacr${deploymentSuffix}'
  location: location
  sku: { name: 'Premium' }
  identity: { type: 'SystemAssigned' }
  properties: {
    adminUserEnabled: false
    publicNetworkAccess: 'Disabled'
    zoneRedundancy: 'Enabled'
    encryption: {
      status: 'enabled'
      keyVaultProperties: {
        keyIdentifier: cmkKeyUri
        identity: runtimeIdentityId
      }
    }
    policies: {
      retentionPolicy:    { days: 30, status: 'enabled' }
      trustPolicy:        { type: 'Notary', status: 'enabled' }
      quarantinePolicy:   { status: 'enabled' }
      exportPolicy:       { status: 'disabled' }
    }
  }
  tags: tags
}

resource acrPullRole 'Microsoft.Authorization/roleAssignments@2022-04-01' = {
  name: guid(acr.id, runtimeIdentityId, 'AcrPull')
  scope: acr
  properties: {
    principalId: runtimePrincipalId
    principalType: 'ServicePrincipal'
    roleDefinitionId: subscriptionResourceId(
      'Microsoft.Authorization/roleDefinitions',
      '7f951dda-4ed3-4680-a7ca-43fe172d538d') // AcrPull
  }
}
Warning — Basic and Standard container registry will not work

The Bicep above will deploy successfully against Basic or Standard container registry, but content trust, geo-replication, and CMK encryption are Premium-only. A Basic-tier deployment passes az deployment validation and fails the first time the Layer 3 promotion pipeline tries to push a signed image. Pin sku: { name: 'Premium' } and reject parameter overrides to lower tiers.

4.3 Microsoft.ManagedIdentity — The Four Identities

Four Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities resources, one for each functional axis of the runtime. These are Microsoft Entra ID service principals; the Bicep creates them and emits their resource IDs and principal IDs as outputs.

IdentityBicep nameGranted (Layer 1)Granted (Layer 2)
runtimemi-apex-merch-runtimeAcrPull on registry · Key Vault Crypto UserStorage Blob Data Reader on per-Service silver/gold
mcpmi-apex-merch-mcpKey Vault Crypto User · Reader on Log AnalyticsBound to MCP tool catalog scoped per Service
ingestmi-apex-merch-ingest—Storage Blob Data Contributor on bronze · Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse Writer
pii-unlockmi-apex-merch-pii-unlockKey Vault Crypto User on PII unwrap keyJust-in-time elevated · audit-attributed only

The four-identity split is deliberate and Microsoft-platform-shaped: AcrPull, Key Vault crypto, data-plane reads, and PII unlock are different blast radii. Microsoft Entra ID conditional-access policies apply per managed identity, not per Container App, so splitting identities is the only way to apply different conditional-access posture to different runtime functions.

BICEP — identity.bicep (excerpt)
var identityNames = [
  'mi-apex-merch-runtime'
  'mi-apex-merch-mcp'
  'mi-apex-merch-ingest'
  'mi-apex-merch-pii-unlock'
]

resource ids 'Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities@2023-01-31' = [for n in identityNames: {
  name: '${n}-${deploymentSuffix}'
  location: location
  tags: tags
}]

output runtimeIdentityId    string = ids[0].id
output runtimePrincipalId   string = ids[0].properties.principalId
output mcpIdentityId        string = ids[1].id
output ingestIdentityId     string = ids[2].id
output piiUnlockIdentityId  string = ids[3].id

4.4 Microsoft.KeyVault — Premium with HSM-Backed CMK

The Layer 1 vault is Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults Premium SKU, RBAC authorization model, soft-delete and purge protection enforced. The CMK is an Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/keys resource of kind RSA-HSM at 2048 bits — Microsoft's HSM-backed key family, FIPS 140-2 Level 3 validated.

BICEP — keyvault.bicep (excerpt)
resource kv 'Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults@2023-07-01' = {
  name: 'apexkv${deploymentSuffix}'
  location: location
  properties: {
    sku: { family: 'A', name: 'premium' }
    tenantId: subscription().tenantId
    enableRbacAuthorization: true
    enableSoftDelete: true
    softDeleteRetentionInDays: 90
    enablePurgeProtection: true
    publicNetworkAccess: 'disabled'
    networkAcls: {
      defaultAction: 'Deny'
      bypass: 'AzureServices'
    }
  }
  tags: tags
}

resource cmk 'Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/keys@2023-07-01' = {
  parent: kv
  name: 'apex-merch-cmk'
  properties: {
    kty: 'RSA-HSM'
    keySize: 2048
    keyOps: [ 'wrapKey', 'unwrapKey', 'encrypt', 'decrypt' ]
    attributes: { enabled: true, exportable: false }
    rotationPolicy: {
      lifetimeActions: [
        { trigger: { timeAfterCreate: 'P365D' }, action: { type: 'Rotate' } }
        { trigger: { timeBeforeExpiry: 'P30D' }, action: { type: 'Notify' } }
      ]
      attributes: { expiryTime: 'P730D' }
    }
  }
}
Independence — Why HSM is non-negotiable

The two-contract model puts the platform on the client's Azure subscription. The CMK that encrypts container registry images, Key Vault secrets, and (eventually) Cosmos DB / Azure SQL data must be a key the client controls cryptographically, not just administratively. RSA-HSM guarantees the private key has never left a Microsoft-validated HSM. A software-protected key would let a Deloitte deployment principal export key material; HSM forecloses that even theoretically. Premium is the only Key Vault SKU that supports HSM-backed keys.

4.5 Microsoft.OperationalInsights and Microsoft.Insights — Azure Monitor Substrate

Layer 1 deploys one Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces per tenant and one Microsoft.Insights/components bound to it. This is the Azure Monitor substrate; every Container App in every Service in the tenant emits to this workspace by default. Microsoft Sentinel attaches to the workspace as a separate resource if the client tenant licenses it.

BICEP — observability.bicep (excerpt)
resource law 'Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces@2023-09-01' = {
  name: 'apex-law-${deploymentSuffix}'
  location: location
  properties: {
    sku: { name: 'PerGB2018' }
    retentionInDays: 30
    features: { enableLogAccessUsingOnlyResourcePermissions: true }
  }
}

resource ai 'Microsoft.Insights/components@2020-02-02' = {
  name: 'apex-ai-${deploymentSuffix}'
  location: location
  kind: 'web'
  properties: {
    Application_Type: 'web'
    WorkspaceResourceId: law.id
    IngestionMode: 'LogAnalytics'
    publicNetworkAccessForIngestion: 'Disabled'
    publicNetworkAccessForQuery:     'Enabled'
  }
}

output logAnalyticsWorkspaceId  string = law.id
output appInsightsConnectionString string = ai.properties.ConnectionString

The WorkspaceResourceId binding is the connecting tissue between Application Insights and Log Analytics; without it, Container Apps telemetry lands in two disconnected stores and Microsoft Sentinel cannot correlate.

4.6 Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments — The Container Apps Environment

The Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments resource is where every Layer 2 Container App lives. It binds to the Layer 1 Log Analytics workspace, declares the workload profiles available to Services in the tenant, and controls the VNet integration story.

BICEP — agents.bicep (managedEnvironments excerpt)
resource env 'Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments@2024-03-01' = {
  name: 'apex-env-${deploymentSuffix}'
  location: location
  properties: {
    appLogsConfiguration: {
      destination: 'log-analytics'
      logAnalyticsConfiguration: {
        customerId: reference(law.id, '2023-09-01').customerId
        sharedKey: listKeys(law.id, '2023-09-01').primarySharedKey
      }
    }
    workloadProfiles: [
      { name: 'Consumption', workloadProfileType: 'Consumption' }
      { name: 'D4',         workloadProfileType: 'D4',          minimumCount: 0, maximumCount: 6 }
    ]
    zoneRedundant: true
    daprAIConnectionString: ai.properties.ConnectionString
  }
}

The Consumption profile is the default for Sprint-1 The Analyst (cost-efficient, scale-to-zero). The Dedicated D4 profile is reserved for higher-burst Services such as those backed by RC-E2E-05 (on-shelf availability) or TMT-TEL-NET-02 (network fault prediction) where steady-state burn dominates. Profiles are part of the Platform layer because they are tenant-wide capacity choices; individual Container Apps select a profile but do not create one.

4.7 The Layer 1 Outputs

Layer 1's job is not just to deploy; its job is to expose a stable contract for Layer 2 to consume. The Sprint 1 main.bicep emits exactly these outputs:

BICEP — main.bicep outputs
output runtimeIdentityId      string = identity.outputs.runtimeIdentityId
output runtimePrincipalId     string = identity.outputs.runtimePrincipalId
output mcpIdentityId          string = identity.outputs.mcpIdentityId
output keyvaultUri            string = keyvault.outputs.kvUri
output cmkKeyUri              string = keyvault.outputs.cmkKeyUri
output acrLoginServer         string = acr.outputs.loginServer
output containerAppsEnvId     string = agents.outputs.envId
output logAnalyticsWorkspaceId string = observability.outputs.logAnalyticsWorkspaceId
output appInsightsConnectionString string = observability.outputs.appInsightsConnectionString

Every Layer 2 Bicep takes these as parameters. That parameter-flow contract — Layer 1 outputs become Layer 2 inputs — is what makes the cake work. If a Service ever creates its own container registry or its own Key Vault, you have collapsed into a one-layer deployment with a Service-shaped duplicate of Platform.

Best Practice

Bicep module outputs from main.bicep are the Microsoft-platform-native way to publish a Platform contract. Resist the urge to write outputs to a parameter file or to Azure App Configuration as a side channel. The Bicep deployment record is the contract; if your Service Bicep can't read it, the Service Bicep is the wrong layer.

Key Takeaways

  • Layer 1 is exactly seven Microsoft Azure resource types, plus role assignments — every one named by its Microsoft resource type and API version.
  • Premium SKUs are mandatory for Microsoft.ContainerRegistry and Microsoft.KeyVault: trust, geo-replication, CMK, and HSM are Premium-only features the architecture requires.
  • Four user-assigned managed identities split runtime, MCP, ingest, and PII-unlock blast radii — this is enforced at the Microsoft Entra ID layer.
  • Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments binds to Log Analytics + Application Insights; Microsoft Sentinel attaches downstream.
  • Layer 1 outputs are the contract Layer 2 consumes — do not duplicate Platform resources inside a Service Bicep.

Deployment Drills

  1. Run az deployment sub what-if against your Lab subscription with the Sprint 1 main.bicep. Confirm the seven Microsoft resource types deploy in the order Container Registry → Managed Identities → Key Vault + Key → Log Analytics + App Insights → Container Apps Environment.
  2. For each Microsoft resource type in §4.1, find the corresponding entry in MVP/docs/deployment.md and confirm the API version pinned in Bicep matches what the inventory documents.
  3. Inspect the role assignment scope on AcrPull — confirm it is scoped to the registry resource, not to the resource group or subscription.
Chapter 5

Identity, Secrets, and Audit Trust

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the Microsoft Entra ID model for the four user-assigned managed identities.
  • Maps secret resolution from Container App env to Microsoft.KeyVault references via the runtime identity.
  • Explains how the LEDGER hash chain anchors to the Microsoft.KeyVault HSM key.
60-second read
  • Microsoft Entra ID user-assigned managed identities are the only authentication path; no client secrets in images.
  • Container App secrets resolve from Microsoft.KeyVault via per-secret identity bindings — runtime · MCP · just-in-time pii-unlock are intentionally separate.
  • The LEDGER hash chain is anchored to the Layer 1 RSA-HSM CMK in Microsoft.KeyVault, inheriting FIPS 140-2 Level 3 trust.
  • Microsoft Defender for Containers · for Key Vault · for App Service are subscription-scope prerequisites · enabled before Layer 1 deploys.

5.1 Microsoft Entra ID — The Identity Boundary

Every runtime call inside a Layer 2 Container App authenticates as a user-assigned managed identity issued by Microsoft Entra ID. There are no client secrets, no service-principal passwords, no federated credentials wired into images. The four identities from §4.3 are the only ways a Container App can call Microsoft.ContainerRegistry, Microsoft.KeyVault, Azure Storage, Microsoft Fabric, or any other Microsoft platform resource.

The Microsoft Entra ID token issued to a user-assigned identity is short-lived (default 1 hour). Containers re-authenticate transparently via the IMDS endpoint at 169.254.169.254 — a Microsoft platform contract documented in the Container Apps managed-identity guide. The agent code does not handle token acquisition; the Microsoft Entra ID SDK in azure-identity (or the language-equivalent) handles it.

5.1.1 Microsoft Entra Agent ID — Agent-Native Identity (GA April 2026)

The four user-assigned managed identities in §4.3 cover resource access — they let a Container App reach Microsoft.ContainerRegistry, Microsoft.KeyVault, Microsoft Fabric, and the rest of the Microsoft platform. They do not, however, give an APEX agent its own identity for purposes of governance, conditional access, or agent-to-agent (A2A) authentication. Until April 2026, that gap was bridged with bespoke per-UAMI Conditional Access policies and ad-hoc OAuth dances.

Microsoft Entra Agent ID closes that gap. Per the Microsoft Learn overview, "Microsoft Entra Agent ID is an identity and security framework that extends Microsoft Entra capabilities to AI agents… The Microsoft Entra Agent identity platform enables developers to create and manage agent identities, which are specialized identity constructs built for AI agents. Agent identity blueprints serve as templates for creating individual agent identities with parent-child relationships, enabling consistent security policies across large numbers of agents."

APEX-M adopts Entra Agent ID as the canonical identity layer for agents in production. The four UAMI identities from §4.3 stay — they remain how the Container App reaches Azure resources. Entra Agent ID sits above them, providing:

  • Blueprint-based provisioning — one parent blueprint per APEX-M tenant (apex-m-tenant-root), one per APEX service (apex-m-rc-e2e-03-blueprint, etc.), and concrete identities per agent role per scenario instantiated from the service blueprint.
  • Policy inheritance — Conditional Access, RBAC, and lifecycle rules attached at the parent blueprint propagate to every child identity. Disable a blueprint and every identity beneath it disables in one operation — critical for incident response and tenant decommissioning.
  • OAuth 2.0 + MCP + A2A — Entra Agent ID supports the standard agent protocols natively, including the on-behalf-of (OBO) flow that propagates an HITL approver's principal through the agent's API calls.
  • Identity governance integration — access reviews, lifecycle management, and orphan-detection apply to agent identities the same way they apply to user identities.

The naming convention is deterministic so the wizard, the audit row, the Microsoft Entra portal, and the Foundry hosted-agent registration all stay in sync (see docs/APEX - Design and Build/agent-identity-blueprints.md §3):

APEX-M agent identity naming
tenant root            : apex-m-tenant-root
service blueprint      : apex-m-{service-code-lowercase}-blueprint
agent identity         : apex-m:{service-code}:{scenario-id}:{role}

Example for The Pricer:
  blueprint            : apex-m-rc-e2e-03-blueprint
  identity             : apex-m:RC-E2E-03:rc-cold-chain-excursion-mid-shift:pricing

Provisioning happens via Microsoft Graph. As of GA, Entra Agent ID resources are not yet exposed as native ARM resource types; the apex-m/infra/bicep/platform/identity.bicep module provisions the four UAMI identities natively and runs a deployment script that calls Microsoft Graph (POST /agentIdentities/blueprints and POST /agentIdentities/blueprints/{id}/instances) for the blueprints. When Microsoft.Graph/agentIdentities resource types land, the deployment script will be replaced with native Bicep resources.

The APEX-Core protocol contract. APEX-Core defines an AgentIdentityProvider protocol (see packages/apex-core/src/apex_core/protocols/agent_identity.py). APEX-M's implementation is apex_m.identity_entra.AgentIdentityProviderEntra, wrapping the Microsoft Graph endpoints. The implementation is variant-isolated — when APEX-G or APEX-A ship, each provides its own concrete impl (Cloud IAM service-account impersonation for APEX-G; IAM Identity Center + STS AssumeRole for APEX-A) against the same protocol. Agent code that uses the protocol works unchanged across all three variants.

Workload Identity Federation for the laptop substrate. Per Chapter 2, the same agent image runs on Docker Desktop locally and Foundry Hosted Agents on Azure. Local substrate uses Workload Identity Federation instead of stored secrets — the federated credential file at $AZURE_FEDERATED_TOKEN_FILE is a child of apex-m-{service-code}-blueprint in Entra Agent ID. Token exchange happens against the Microsoft identity platform; no static credential lives on the laptop. This satisfies the Microsoft Cloud Security Benchmark v2 AI-7 control (no long-lived secrets in development environments).

Best Practice — Provision blueprints before agents

The wizard's deployment sequence creates the tenant root blueprint, then service blueprints, then per-agent identities — in that order. Skipping a level (e.g., creating an agent identity directly without a service blueprint) is rejected by the deployment script and the wizard's render endpoint. The hierarchy is the entire policy-inheritance argument; flat identity provisioning forfeits it.

For the full blueprint hierarchy, per-service policy table, decommissioning sequence, and APEX-Core protocol mapping, see docs/APEX - Design and Build/agent-identity-blueprints.md.

5.2 Secret Resolution via Microsoft.App secretRef

Secrets reach the Container App via two mechanisms, both Microsoft-native:

5.2.1 Microsoft.KeyVault references in Container App env

The Microsoft.App/containerApps resource exposes a secrets array, where each entry can pull from a Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/secrets by URI under the runtime identity:

BICEP — Container App secret pull
secrets: [
  {
    name: 'ledger-dsn'
    keyVaultUrl: '${keyvaultUri}secrets/ledger-dsn'
    identity: runtimeIdentityId
  }
  {
    name: 'mcp-bootstrap-token'
    keyVaultUrl: '${keyvaultUri}secrets/mcp-bootstrap-token'
    identity: mcpIdentityId
  }
]

Each secret is resolved by the identity declared on the secret entry — runtime for the LEDGER DSN, MCP for the MCP bootstrap token. The identities are not interchangeable; the Microsoft.Authorization role assignments in §4.1 ensure each can only resolve secrets in its own scope.

5.2.2 Just-in-time PII unlock

The fourth identity — mi-apex-merch-pii-unlock — is intentionally not bound to the Container App at deployment time. It is acquired at runtime via Microsoft Entra ID's acquireTokenForUserAssignedIdentity only when an audit-attributed PII unwrap is required. The acquisition itself writes a LEDGER row before the unwrap fires.

Warning — Do not co-mount PII unlock

If you bind mi-apex-merch-pii-unlock as a standing identity on a Container App, every replica gets standing unwrap permission. The just-in-time pattern is the entire security argument for splitting it from mi-apex-merch-runtime. Confirm in agents.bicep that the Container App's identity: block lists only runtime + MCP, not PII unlock.

5.3 The LEDGER and the Microsoft.KeyVault HSM Anchor

The MVP's LEDGER is a WORM (write-once, read-many) audit table — every agent decision, every HITL approval, every harness step generates a row. Sprint 1 added two columns: _hash and _prev_hash, computed by compute_row_hash in mvp_test_harness.runtime.ledger. The chain is verified by verify_chain, which returns a ChainResult(ok, broken_at, message).

The Microsoft platform piece: the seed of the chain — and, in PROD, periodic checkpoint rows — are signed using the Layer 1 CMK in Microsoft.KeyVault. The CMK is HSM-backed (RSA-HSM 2048), so the audit chain inherits the FIPS 140-2 Level 3 trust posture of the Microsoft-validated HSM. A LEDGER tamper attempt that bypasses the application path still has to forge an HSM signature, which is cryptographically infeasible.

PYTHON — LEDGER signing call (sketch)
from azure.identity import DefaultAzureCredential
from azure.keyvault.keys.crypto import CryptographyClient, SignatureAlgorithm

credential = DefaultAzureCredential()  # picks up runtime managed identity
crypto = CryptographyClient(key_id=cmk_key_uri, credential=credential)
sig = crypto.sign(SignatureAlgorithm.rs256, checkpoint_hash_bytes)
# write checkpoint row with sig column populated

Microsoft.KeyVault rate-limits HSM signing aggressively (Premium tier: hundreds of ops/sec, not thousands). For this reason, the LEDGER signs checkpoints — typically every Nth row or every minute — not every row. The chain links each row to its predecessor; the checkpoints anchor the chain to HSM-backed truth.

5.4 Microsoft Defender for Cloud and Microsoft Defender for Containers

Microsoft Defender for Cloud should be enabled at subscription scope on the client tenant before any Layer 1 deployment. Specifically:

  • Microsoft Defender for Containers — scans Microsoft.ContainerRegistry images on push and on pull; flags vulnerabilities in the image and its base layers; integrates with the Notation trust pipeline.
  • Microsoft Defender for Key Vault — alerts on anomalous Key Vault operations (mass secret read, unusual unwrap patterns).
  • Microsoft Defender for App Service — covers Microsoft.App/containerApps as part of its 2025 expansion.

These do not appear in the Layer 1 Bicep because they are subscription-scope toggles, not resource-scope deployments. Appendix B's Day-Zero checklist starts with confirming Defender for Containers is enabled on the target subscription.

Key Takeaways

  • Microsoft Entra ID user-assigned managed identities are the only authentication path; no client secrets are wired into images.
  • Container App secrets resolve from Microsoft.KeyVault via per-secret identity bindings — runtime, MCP, and just-in-time PII unlock are intentionally separate.
  • The LEDGER hash chain is anchored to the Layer 1 RSA-HSM CMK in Microsoft.KeyVault, inheriting FIPS 140-2 Level 3 trust.
  • Microsoft Defender for Containers + for Key Vault + for App Service are subscription-scope prerequisites, set before Layer 1 deploys.

Deployment Drills

  1. List every secret your Container App reads. For each, name the identity that resolves it. If any secret is reachable by more than one identity, narrow the role assignment.
  2. Confirm Microsoft Defender for Containers and for Key Vault are enabled at subscription scope on your Lab subscription. Land any gap before next deployment.
  3. Find the LEDGER checkpoint cadence in the harness config. Compare against Microsoft.KeyVault Premium HSM rate limits — confirm you have headroom of at least 10×.
Chapter 5A

Deploying Microsoft Entra ID & Purview

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the deployment of every Entra ID primitive APEX-M depends on — app registrations, managed identities, custom roles, Conditional Access policies, PIM eligibility, ID Governance access reviews — with Bicep where supported and Microsoft Graph PowerShell SDK where Bicep is not yet available.
  • Walks Microsoft Purview deployment — Microsoft.Purview/accounts Bicep, data-source registration, scan rule sets, sensitivity-label rollout, DLP-policy deployment, DSPM for AI configuration.
  • Maps the per-tier identity envelope (Lab v0.1 / DEV / STAGE / PROD) including which Conditional Access policies are required at which tier, and the Foundry Hosted Agent identity flow that bridges Layer 1 platform identity to per-published-agent identities.
  • Establishes the network-isolation, naming, and promotion model that pairs Entra and Purview deployments with the rest of the APEX-M Bicep blueprint.
90-second read
  • Entra primitives deploy via Microsoft Graph (Bicep coverage growing): app registrations · service principals · managed identities · custom roles · Conditional Access policies · PIM role-eligibility settings · access-package definitions.
  • Microsoft Graph Bicep extension (preview) lets you deploy Microsoft.Graph/applications, Microsoft.Graph/servicePrincipals, and groups via Bicep. Conditional Access and PIM still require Graph PowerShell SDK as of this writing.
  • Managed identities (UAMI) are pure Bicep (Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities@2023-01-31); APEX-M ships four standard UAMIs per Service tenant: runtime · mcp · ingest · pii-unlock.
  • Foundry Hosted Agent identities are Foundry-issued at agent-publication time; APEX-M Bicep grants per-Service role assignments to the published agentIdentityId (per Ch 11.5.6).
  • Purview account is Bicep (Microsoft.Purview/accounts@2024-04-01-preview); data-source registration, scans, classifications, sensitivity labels, DLP, Insider Risk policies are Graph / Purview REST.
  • Microsoft Fabric tenant must be explicitly registered in Purview — APEX-M ships deployment-scripts/purview-fabric-register.ps1 as a STAGE+ promotion gate.
  • Network isolation: Purview private endpoint per region · Entra Conditional Access location filters · Workload ID Premium for service-principal CA · Continuous Access Evaluation for high-frequency token-refresh paths.

5A.1 Bicep for Entra ID — What Is Native, What Is Graph

Entra ID resource coverage in Bicep is split across two providers:

ResourceBicep providerAPI version
User-assigned managed identityMicrosoft.ManagedIdentity2023-01-31
System-assigned managed identityInline on the host resource (Container App · Function App · etc.)n/a — set identity: { type: 'SystemAssigned' }

Service principalMicrosoft.Graph (preview)v1.0
Group · group membershipMicrosoft.Graph (preview)v1.0
Federated identity credentialMicrosoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities/federatedIdentityCredentials2023-01-31

APEX-M's Bicep blueprint does what Bicep covers; deployment scripts (PowerShell modules wrapping Microsoft Graph SDK) cover the rest, invoked by Bicep deploymentScripts with the platform UAMI as the executor.

5A.2 Managed Identities — Four Standard UAMIs per Service Tenant

Per Deployment Guide §4.3, APEX-M provisions four user-assigned managed identities per Service tenant. Bicep:

infra/bicep/platform/identity.bicep · four standard UAMIs
@description('Engagement code, e.g., apex-tenant-rc-wave1')
param engagementCode string
@description('Region')
param location string = resourceGroup().location

resource runtimeIdentity 'Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities@2023-01-31' = {
  name: 'mi-${engagementCode}-runtime'
  location: location
}
resource mcpIdentity 'Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities@2023-01-31' = {
  name: 'mi-${engagementCode}-mcp'
  location: location
}
resource ingestIdentity 'Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities@2023-01-31' = {
  name: 'mi-${engagementCode}-ingest'
  location: location
}
resource piiUnlockIdentity 'Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities@2023-01-31' = {
  name: 'mi-${engagementCode}-pii-unlock'
  location: location
}

output runtimePrincipalId string = runtimeIdentity.properties.principalId
output mcpPrincipalId string = mcpIdentity.properties.principalId
output ingestPrincipalId string = ingestIdentity.properties.principalId
output piiUnlockPrincipalId string = piiUnlockIdentity.properties.principalId

Federated identity credentials (for GitHub Actions or external workload federation) attach as a child resource:

infra/bicep/platform/identity.bicep · GitHub Actions federation
resource githubFederation 'Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities/federatedIdentityCredentials@2023-01-31' = {
  parent: runtimeIdentity
  name: 'github-actions-main'
  properties: {
    audiences: ['api://AzureADTokenExchange']
    issuer: 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    subject: 'repo:contoso/apex-m-rc-e2e-03:ref:refs/heads/main'
  }
}

5A.3 App Registrations via Microsoft Graph Bicep Extension

The Microsoft Graph Bicep extension (preview) lets app registrations live in Bicep alongside the rest of the Layer 1 platform. Reference the extension at the top of the Bicep file:

infra/bicep/platform/app-registrations.bicep · APEX MCP host app reg
extension microsoftGraphV1

@description('Engagement code')
param engagementCode string

resource apexMcpApp 'Microsoft.Graph/applications@v1.0' = {
  uniqueName: 'apex-mcp-host-${engagementCode}'
  displayName: 'APEX-M MCP Host (${engagementCode})'
  description: 'Workload identity for the APEX-M MCP host. Single-tenant. Requires Workload ID Premium for Conditional Access.'
  signInAudience: 'AzureADMyOrg'
  api: {
    requestedAccessTokenVersion: 2
    oauth2PermissionScopes: [
      {
        id: guid(engagementCode, 'mcp.read')
        type: 'User'
        adminConsentDisplayName: 'Read APEX MCP Gold marts'
        adminConsentDescription: 'Allow signed-in user to read APEX MCP Gold marts via OBO flow.'
        userConsentDisplayName: 'Read APEX Gold data on your behalf'
        userConsentDescription: 'The app will read APEX Gold data on your behalf.'
        value: 'mcp.read'
        isEnabled: true
      }
    ]
  }
}

resource apexMcpSp 'Microsoft.Graph/servicePrincipals@v1.0' = {
  appId: apexMcpApp.appId
  displayName: apexMcpApp.displayName
}

output appId string = apexMcpApp.appId
output appObjectId string = apexMcpApp.id
output spObjectId string = apexMcpSp.id

5A.4 Foundry-Issued Agent Identity Lifecycle

Foundry agent identities are not deployed by APEX-M Bicep — they are auto-provisioned by Foundry at agent publication time (per Services Guide §10.5.3). What APEX-M Bicep does:

  1. Wait for the published agent's agentIdentityId output from the Foundry agent resource (per Deployment Guide §11.5.7).
  2. Grant per-Service role assignments to that agentIdentityId: Storage Blob Data Reader on bronze · Microsoft.Cache/Redis User on the Service Redis · Key Vault Secrets User scoped to the Service's secret set · Reader on the Microsoft Fabric workspace.
  3. Apply OneLake security policies (per Deployment Guide §6A.5) restricting the agent identity to its Service's tables/columns/rows.

The agent identity blueprint persists for the lifetime of the Foundry project (or agent application). Deletion of the Foundry project removes the blueprint and shared identity; deletion of an agent application removes its distinct identity. APEX-M's tenant-offboarding playbook destroys agents → projects → Foundry resource group in that order to ensure clean Entra cleanup.

5A.5 Conditional Access Policy Deployment

Conditional Access policies are deployed via Microsoft Graph PowerShell SDK invoked from a Bicep deploymentScripts resource. APEX-M's baseline policies:

deployment-scripts/conditional-access-baseline.ps1 · CA policies
param([string]$EngagementCode)

Connect-MgGraph -Identity -Scopes 'Policy.ReadWrite.ConditionalAccess'

# 1. Block legacy auth tenant-wide
$blockLegacy = @{
  displayName = "${EngagementCode}-CA-001-block-legacy-auth"
  state = 'enabled'
  conditions = @{
    clientAppTypes = @('exchangeActiveSync', 'other')
    users = @{ includeUsers = @('All'); excludeRoles = @('Global Administrator') }
    applications = @{ includeApplications = @('All') }
  }
  grantControls = @{ operator = 'OR'; builtInControls = @('block') }
}
New-MgIdentityConditionalAccessPolicy -BodyParameter $blockLegacy

# 2. Require MFA for all users
$requireMfa = @{
  displayName = "${EngagementCode}-CA-002-require-mfa-all-users"
  state = 'enabled'
  conditions = @{
    users = @{ includeUsers = @('All'); excludeRoles = @('Global Administrator') }
    applications = @{ includeApplications = @('All') }
  }
  grantControls = @{ operator = 'OR'; builtInControls = @('mfa') }
}
New-MgIdentityConditionalAccessPolicy -BodyParameter $requireMfa

# 3. Block APEX MCP service principal from outside known IP ranges
# Requires Workload Identities Premium licence
$blockMcpExternalIp = @{
  displayName = "${EngagementCode}-CA-010-mcp-block-external-ip"
  state = 'enabled'
  conditions = @{
    users = @{ includeServicePrincipals = @($mcpSpObjectId) }
    locations = @{ includeLocations = @('All'); excludeLocations = @($apexPlatformIpRangeId) }
    applications = @{ includeApplications = @('All') }
  }
  grantControls = @{ operator = 'OR'; builtInControls = @('block') }
}
New-MgIdentityConditionalAccessPolicy -BodyParameter $blockMcpExternalIp

# 4. Block APEX MCP service principal on workload identity risk = high
$blockMcpRisk = @{
  displayName = "${EngagementCode}-CA-011-mcp-block-on-risk"
  state = 'enabled'
  conditions = @{
    users = @{ includeServicePrincipals = @($mcpSpObjectId) }
    servicePrincipalRiskLevels = @('high')
    applications = @{ includeApplications = @('All') }
  }
  grantControls = @{ operator = 'OR'; builtInControls = @('block') }
}
New-MgIdentityConditionalAccessPolicy -BodyParameter $blockMcpRisk

# 5. Restrict agent identities to apex platform VNet (preview · agent-id condition)
# See https://learn.microsoft.com/entra/identity/conditional-access/agent-id
$blockAgentExternalIp = @{
  displayName = "${EngagementCode}-CA-020-agent-restrict-vnet"
  state = 'enabled'
  conditions = @{
    users = @{ includeAgents = 'AllAgents' }
    locations = @{ includeLocations = @('All'); excludeLocations = @($apexPlatformIpRangeId) }
    applications = @{ includeApplications = @('All') }
  }
  grantControls = @{ operator = 'OR'; builtInControls = @('block') }
}
New-MgIdentityConditionalAccessPolicy -BodyParameter $blockAgentExternalIp

Every policy lands in report-only mode first; APEX-M's promotion gate flips state to enabled only after one week of clean impact analysis in Sentinel.

5A.6 PIM Role Eligibility Configuration

PIM eligibility settings — eligible-vs-active assignments, activation duration, approval requirements, MFA requirements — are deployed via Graph PowerShell:

deployment-scripts/pim-eligibility.ps1 · PIM role config
param([string]$EngagementCode, [string]$PrincipalId, [string]$RoleId)

Connect-MgGraph -Identity -Scopes 'RoleManagement.ReadWrite.Directory'

# Make the principal eligible (not active) for the role
$eligibility = @{
  action = 'AdminAssign'
  justification = "${EngagementCode} PIM eligibility per APEX-M baseline"
  roleDefinitionId = $RoleId
  directoryScopeId = '/'
  principalId = $PrincipalId
  scheduleInfo = @{
    startDateTime = (Get-Date).ToString('yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ssZ')
    expiration = @{ type = 'noExpiration' }
  }
}
New-MgRoleManagementDirectoryRoleEligibilityScheduleRequest -BodyParameter $eligibility

# Configure activation rules (max duration, approval required, justification required)
$activationRules = @{
  isExpirationRequired = $true
  maximumDuration = 'PT4H'         # 4-hour max activation
  enableApprovalRequired = $true
  approvalSettings = @{
    isApprovalRequired = $true
    isApprovalRequiredForExtension = $true
    approvalStages = @(
      @{
        approvalStageTimeOutInDays = 1
        isApproverJustificationRequired = $true
        primaryApprovers = @(
          @{ '@odata.type' = '#microsoft.graph.singleUser'; userId = $approverObjectId }
        )
      }
    )
  }
  isMfaRequired = $true
  isJustificationRequired = $true
  isTicketingRequired = $true
}
Update-MgPolicyRoleManagementPolicy -PolicyId $policyId -BodyParameter $activationRules

5A.7 Microsoft Purview Account — Bicep

The Purview account is the entry point. One per APEX-M tenant (regional · per-environment):

infra/bicep/platform/purview.bicep · Purview account
@description('Engagement code')
param engagementCode string
@description('Tier: lab | dev | stage | prod')
param tier string
@description('Region — must match platform region')
param location string = resourceGroup().location

resource purviewAccount 'Microsoft.Purview/accounts@2024-04-01-preview' = {
  name: 'pv-${engagementCode}-${tier}'
  location: location
  identity: {
    type: 'SystemAssigned'
  }
  properties: {
    publicNetworkAccess: tier == 'prod' ? 'Disabled' : 'Enabled'
    managedResourceGroupName: 'rg-pv-${engagementCode}-${tier}-managed'
  }
  sku: {
    name: 'Standard'
    capacity: tier == 'prod' ? 4 : 1
  }
  tags: {
    'apex.tier': tier
    'apex.workload': 'governance'
  }
}

output purviewId string = purviewAccount.id
output purviewName string = purviewAccount.name
output purviewSystemAssignedPrincipalId string = purviewAccount.identity.principalId

Per the Microsoft Learn Purview reliability guide, Purview's system-assigned managed identity is what authenticates scans against data sources. The Bicep above grants it Reader on the Service's storage accounts and Microsoft Fabric workspaces (or for sources that don't support managed identity, APEX-M provisions a Microsoft.KeyVault secret with the connection string and grants Purview's managed identity Get-Secret on it).

5A.8 Data-Source Registration & Scan Rules

Data sources are registered via Purview REST. APEX-M's per-Service registration script:

deployment-scripts/purview-register-source.ps1 · per-Service source
param(
  [string]$PurviewAccountName,
  [string]$ServiceCode,
  [string]$FabricWorkspaceId,
  [string]$BronzeStorageAccountId
)

$purviewToken = (Get-AzAccessToken -ResourceUrl 'https://purview.azure.net').Token
$base = "https://${PurviewAccountName}.purview.azure.com"

# 1. Register the Microsoft Fabric workspace as a Purview data source
$fabricSource = @{
  kind = 'PowerBI'   # Fabric tenant registration uses the PowerBI source kind
  properties = @{
    tenant = (Get-MgContext).TenantId
    collection = @{
      type = 'CollectionReference'
      referenceName = "apex-${ServiceCode}"
    }
  }
} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6

Invoke-RestMethod -Method PUT `
  -Uri "${base}/scan/datasources/apex-${ServiceCode}-fabric?api-version=2023-09-01" `
  -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $purviewToken" } `
  -Body $fabricSource -ContentType 'application/json'

# 2. Register the Bronze storage account as a Purview data source
$storageSource = @{
  kind = 'AzureStorage'
  properties = @{
    resourceId = $BronzeStorageAccountId
    collection = @{
      type = 'CollectionReference'
      referenceName = "apex-${ServiceCode}"
    }
  }
} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
Invoke-RestMethod -Method PUT `
  -Uri "${base}/scan/datasources/apex-${ServiceCode}-bronze?api-version=2023-09-01" `
  -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $purviewToken" } `
  -Body $storageSource -ContentType 'application/json'

# 3. Create a scan rule set with system + custom classifications
$scanRuleSet = @{
  kind = 'AzureStorage'
  properties = @{
    classificationRuleNames = @(
      'MICROSOFT.PII.US.SSN',
      'MICROSOFT.PII.US.CREDITCARD',
      'MICROSOFT.HEALTH.US.NPI',
      "apex-${ServiceCode}-tier3-phi-pattern"   # custom SIT
    )
  }
} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
Invoke-RestMethod -Method PUT `
  -Uri "${base}/scan/scanrulesets/apex-${ServiceCode}-rules?api-version=2023-09-01" `
  -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $purviewToken" } `
  -Body $scanRuleSet -ContentType 'application/json'

# 4. Create a scan with weekly trigger
$scan = @{
  kind = 'AzureStorageMsi'   # use Purview's managed identity
  properties = @{
    scanRulesetName = "apex-${ServiceCode}-rules"
    scanRulesetType = 'Custom'
    collection = @{
      type = 'CollectionReference'
      referenceName = "apex-${ServiceCode}"
    }
    schedule = @{
      recurrence = @{
        frequency = 'Week'; interval = 1
      }
      startTime = (Get-Date).AddDays(1).ToString('yyyy-MM-ddT02:00:00Z')
    }
  }
} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
Invoke-RestMethod -Method PUT `
  -Uri "${base}/scan/datasources/apex-${ServiceCode}-bronze/scans/weekly-scan?api-version=2023-09-01" `
  -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $purviewToken" } `
  -Body $scan -ContentType 'application/json'
Warning — Microsoft Fabric tenant must be explicitly registered

OneLake is not automatically included in Purview's Data Map. Per Register and scan Microsoft Fabric, the Fabric tenant registration is a separate, explicit one-time action per environment. APEX-M's STAGE-promotion gate deployment-scripts/purview-fabric-register.ps1 runs this registration and verifies it before allowing further promotion.

5A.9 Sensitivity Label Deployment

Sensitivity labels are deployed via the Microsoft Purview / Microsoft 365 Compliance Center · PowerShell. Once published, they appear in Office apps, Microsoft Fabric items, and Purview Data Map:

deployment-scripts/sensitivity-labels.ps1 · APEX-M baseline
param([string]$EngagementCode)

Connect-IPPSSession   # Security & Compliance PowerShell

# 1. Create the parent label
New-Label -Name 'apex-confidential-engagement' `
  -DisplayName 'Confidential — Engagement' `
  -Tooltip 'Engagement-internal documents · APEX-M deliverables · Bicep parameter sets' `
  -EncryptionEnabled $true `
  -EncryptionProtectionType 'Template' `
  -EncryptionRightsDefinitions @(
    @{ Identity = "engagement-${EngagementCode}@contoso.com"; Rights = 'VIEW,EDIT,EXTRACT' }
  )

# 2. Tier-3 PHI (HLS engagements only)
New-Label -Name 'apex-confidential-phi' `
  -DisplayName 'Confidential — Client PHI' `
  -EncryptionEnabled $true `
  -EncryptionProtectionType 'Template' `
  -EncryptionRightsDefinitions @(
    @{ Identity = "clinical-reviewers-${EngagementCode}@contoso.com"; Rights = 'VIEW,EXTRACT' }
    @{ Identity = "agent-the-pricer-rc-e2e-03-prod@contoso.onmicrosoft.com"; Rights = 'VIEW,EXTRACT' }
    @{ Identity = "mi-${EngagementCode}-pii-unlock@contoso.onmicrosoft.com"; Rights = 'VIEW,EXTRACT' }
  )

# 3. Publish a label policy applying to the engagement group
New-LabelPolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-policy" `
  -Labels @('apex-confidential-engagement', 'apex-confidential-phi') `
  -ExchangeLocation 'All' -SharePointLocation 'All' -OneDriveLocation 'All' `
  -ModernGroupLocation 'All'

Per Services Guide §10.5.11, agent identity rights must be enumerated explicitly on PHI/financial labels — generic "all signed-in users" or "any authenticated user" rights do not include agent identities.

5A.10 DLP Policy Deployment

DLP policies are deployed via Microsoft 365 Compliance PowerShell. APEX-M's required PROD DLP policies:

deployment-scripts/dlp-baseline.ps1 · APEX-M required DLP
param([string]$EngagementCode)

Connect-IPPSSession

# 1. Endpoint DLP — block paste of Tier-3 PHI into third-party AI sites
New-DlpCompliancePolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-endpoint-ai-paste" `
  -EndpointDlpLocation 'All'

New-DlpComplianceRule -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-block-ai-paste-phi" `
  -Policy "apex-${EngagementCode}-endpoint-ai-paste" `
  -ContentContainsSensitiveInformation @(
    @{ name = 'U.S. Patient Identifier'; minCount = '1' },
    @{ name = "apex-${EngagementCode}-tier3-phi-pattern"; minCount = '1' }
  ) `
  -EndpointDlpRestrictions @(
    @{ Setting = 'CopyPasteToBrowser'; Value = 'Block'; Domains = @('chat.openai.com', 'claude.ai', 'gemini.google.com', 'bard.google.com') }
  ) `
  -BlockAccess $true

# 2. Microsoft 365 Copilot — restrict prompts containing Tier-3 PHI
# (Only allow when the Service context is the appropriate one)
New-DlpCompliancePolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-copilot-phi-fence" `
  -CopilotLocation @('All')
New-DlpComplianceRule -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-copilot-phi-restrict" `
  -Policy "apex-${EngagementCode}-copilot-phi-fence" `
  -ContentContainsSensitiveInformation @(
    @{ name = "apex-${EngagementCode}-tier3-phi-pattern"; minCount = '1' }
  ) `
  -BlockAccess $true `
  -BlockAccessScope 'All'

# 3. Teams — block adaptive cards with raw Tier-3 PHI
New-DlpCompliancePolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-teams-phi" `
  -TeamsLocation @('All')
New-DlpComplianceRule -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-teams-phi-block" `
  -Policy "apex-${EngagementCode}-teams-phi" `
  -ContentContainsSensitiveInformation @(
    @{ name = "apex-${EngagementCode}-tier3-phi-pattern"; minCount = '1' }
  ) `
  -BlockAccess $true

5A.11 Insider Risk Management Policy Deployment

Insider Risk Management policies use templated detections — data theft by departing users · data leak · risky AI usage. Deployed via Microsoft 365 Compliance PowerShell:

deployment-scripts/insider-risk-baseline.ps1 · IRM policies
param([string]$EngagementCode)

Connect-IPPSSession

# 1. Data leak — Tier-3/4 priority content
New-InsiderRiskPolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-data-leak-tier3-4" `
  -InsiderRiskScenario 'DataLeaks' `
  -PriorityContentSensitivityLabels @(
    'apex-confidential-phi',
    'apex-highly-confidential-financial'
  ) `
  -Indicators @(
    'CopyToUSBDevice',
    'PrintFile',
    'CopyToCloudPersonal',
    'AIUsageGenerative'   # the AI-specific indicator
  )

# 2. Departing user — boost monitoring 30 days before HR last-day signal
New-InsiderRiskPolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-departing-user" `
  -InsiderRiskScenario 'DepartingUserDataTheft' `
  -ScopeLookback 30

# 3. Risky AI usage policy
New-InsiderRiskPolicy -Name "apex-${EngagementCode}-risky-ai-usage" `
  -InsiderRiskScenario 'RiskyAIUsage' `
  -Indicators @('AIUsageGenerative', 'AIUsagePromptSensitive')

5A.12 DSPM for AI Configuration

Microsoft Purview now ships two DSPM-for-AI surfaces — the new DSPM for AI experience (default for new tenants) and the original classic DSPM for AI (still available for tenants that have not migrated). APEX-M targets the new experience because it adds agent-specific signals that pair with Microsoft Entra ID Protection's agent risk events (unfamiliarResourceAccess, signInSpike, failedAccessAttempt, riskyUserSignIn, threatIntelligenceAccount) — which is the data feed the Layer 1 Sentinel + Defender wiring in Ch 6B subscribes to.

New DSPM for AI (APEX-M default)Classic DSPM for AI
Agent inventoryYes — surfaces Foundry hosted agents, Copilot Studio agents, Microsoft 365 Agents, third-party MCP-connected agentsNo — discovery limited to Copilot for M365 + tenant Copilot Studio
Agent risk correlationCorrelates with Entra ID Protection agent risk events; per-agent risk score in Activity ExplorerUser-level risk only
Sensitive interactionsPer-agent sensitive-prompt / sensitive-response counts; pivot by Service code via Purview tagsPer-user sensitive-prompt counts only
Recommendations / one-click policiesRefreshed recommendation engine (DSPM-for-AI v2) covering both human and agent usersOriginal recommendation set, human users only
API accessMicrosoft Graph DSPM-for-AI APIs (preview at the time of this writing — check Microsoft Learn for GA)Portal only

APEX-M PROD checklist for the new DSPM for AI:

  • In the Purview portal, switch the tenant toggle from "Classic DSPM for AI" to "DSPM for AI" (the new experience). For tenants already on the new experience this is a no-op.
  • Enable DSPM for AI · ensure unified audit log captures Microsoft 365 Copilot · Microsoft Foundry · Microsoft Copilot Studio · Microsoft Security Copilot · Microsoft Agent 365 · Copilot in Fabric activities · plus the per-agent activities surfaced by the new experience (Foundry hosted-agent invocations · agent identity sign-ins · agent tool calls).
  • Configure Activity Explorer to show the AI activities tab and the new Agents tab · grant compliance team Reader access via the apex-${EngagementCode}-compliance-readers group.
  • Configure label inheritance so Microsoft 365 Copilot-generated content inherits highest-priority sensitivity label (this is a tenant-level Purview setting; APEX-M's tenant-onboarding playbook turns it on).
  • Verify Endpoint DLP has the block-AI-paste rules from §5A.10 · verify users see the block on a synthetic test paste of a Tier-3 pattern into chat.openai.com.
  • Wire agent risk events to Sentinel. In Microsoft Entra ID Protection, enable agent risk-event collection; in the Sentinel Defender XDR connector, confirm the AIAgentsInfo table is populating (per Ch 6B). DSPM for AI's agent inventory and Sentinel's agent telemetry pair to give the SOC a per-agent view rather than per-user.
Note — tenants still on classic DSPM for AI

Some engagement tenants will not yet be on the new DSPM for AI experience — Microsoft is rolling tenants forward in waves. If a client tenant is still on classic, APEX-M's onboarding playbook captures this as a Layer 0 readiness item: open a request with the client's Microsoft FastTrack contact to schedule migration, and ship the engagement against classic in the interim. Most classic items in the checklist above still work; the agent-specific items do not.

5A.13 Network Isolation

Per Purview private link, Purview supports private endpoints for both account access and ingestion endpoints. APEX-M PROD Bicep:

infra/bicep/platform/purview-private-endpoints.bicep · STAGE/PROD only
param purviewId string
param vnetId string
param subnetId string
param location string = resourceGroup().location

resource purviewPe 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints@2023-11-01' = {
  name: 'pe-pv-account'
  location: location
  properties: {
    subnet: { id: subnetId }
    privateLinkServiceConnections: [
      {
        name: 'pv-account'
        properties: {
          privateLinkServiceId: purviewId
          groupIds: ['account']
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}

resource purviewPortalPe 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints@2023-11-01' = {
  name: 'pe-pv-portal'
  location: location
  properties: {
    subnet: { id: subnetId }
    privateLinkServiceConnections: [
      {
        name: 'pv-portal'
        properties: {
          privateLinkServiceId: purviewId
          groupIds: ['portal']
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}

resource purviewIngestionPe 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints@2023-11-01' = {
  name: 'pe-pv-ingestion'
  location: location
  properties: {
    subnet: { id: subnetId }
    privateLinkServiceConnections: [
      {
        name: 'pv-ingestion'
        properties: {
          privateLinkServiceId: purviewId
          groupIds: ['ingestion']
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}

For Entra ID, network isolation is achieved through Conditional Access location filters (per §5A.5) rather than network primitives — Entra is a global service. The CA policies that scope service principals and agent identities to the apex-m platform IP range are the moral equivalent of a private endpoint.

5A.14 Promotion — Across Lab → DEV → STAGE → PROD

Per-tier configuration matrix:

TierEntraPurview
LaptopLocal Docker Compose · no EntraNone — Bronze/Silver/Gold are local Postgres + DuckDB
Lab v0.1Single tenant · 4 UAMIs · simple CA (MFA + block legacy auth) · no PIMPv account (Standard 1 capacity) · public ingestion endpoint · no scans yet
DEVSame tenant or per-engagement DEV tenant · all baseline CA · Workload ID Premium for MCP host SP CAPv (Standard 1) · public access · Bronze/Silver scans on schedule · classifications + sensitivity labels
STAGEPer-engagement tenant · full CA library incl. agent-id policies · PIM eligibility for break-glass roles · access reviews quarterlyPv (Standard 4) · private endpoints for ingestion only · Microsoft Fabric tenant registration · DLP policies in monitor mode · IRM in monitor mode
PRODPer-engagement tenant · all CA enforced (no report-only) · PIM with dual-control approvers · CAE enabled for all eligible workload identities · ID Protection wired to SentinelPv (Standard 4+) · all private endpoints · public access disabled · DLP enforced · IRM enforced · DSPM for AI enabled · all sensitivity labels published · scans on weekly schedule + on-change triggers
