  • Services Guide: Ch 10A — Microsoft Entra ID & Purview Deep Dive · Ch 7 — PII Classification · §30.1.5 — Trust Envelope

Key Takeaways

  • Entra ID resources split between Bicep and Microsoft Graph: managed identities + federated credentials are pure Bicep; app registrations + service principals + groups use the Graph Bicep extension; Conditional Access + PIM + access reviews are Graph PowerShell.
  • APEX-M provisions four standard UAMIs per Service tenant: runtime · mcp · ingest · pii-unlock. Federated identity credentials replace client secrets for GitHub Actions and Foundry agent blueprints.
  • Foundry-issued agent identities are auto-provisioned at agent publication; APEX-M Bicep grants per-Service role assignments to the published agentIdentityId.
  • CA policies deploy in report-only first, flip to enabled after a clean week of impact analysis. Workload ID Premium licence required to scope CA to APEX MCP host service principals; agent-id CA is preview.
  • Microsoft Purview account is Bicep (Microsoft.Purview/accounts@2024-04-01-preview); data sources, scans, classifications, sensitivity labels, DLP, IRM are Purview/Compliance REST + PowerShell.
  • Microsoft Fabric tenant must be explicitly registered in Purview · this is a STAGE-promotion gate.
  • STAGE+ deploys private endpoints for Purview account, portal, and ingestion; Entra network isolation is achieved via CA location filters since Entra is a global service.
  • Sensitivity labels for Tier-3/4 content must enumerate agent identities explicitly — generic "all users" or "all authenticated" rights do not include agents.

Deployment Drills

  1. Deploy the four standard UAMIs into a Lab v0.1 resource group. Verify each has the correct principal ID output and a clean role-assignment graph.
  2. Run the CA baseline script in report-only mode for one week. Inspect the Sign-in Logs · Conditional Access tab for unexpected impact. Tune exclusions; flip to enabled.
  3. Deploy the Purview account. Register the Microsoft Fabric tenant. Scan one Bronze storage account. Inspect the classified assets for Tier-3 PHI patterns.
  4. Publish the sensitivity-label baseline. Apply apex-confidential-phi to one Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse table. Verify the agent identity (mi-pii-unlock) can read the table; verify a non-agent user cannot.
  5. Test Endpoint DLP: from a Windows endpoint onboarded to Purview, paste a synthetic Tier-3 pattern into chat.openai.com. Confirm the block fires and the activity surfaces in DSPM for AI Activity Explorer.
  6. Run the Insider Risk baseline. Trigger the synthetic departing-user signal. Verify the policy elevates monitoring for the test user; confirm no false positives in 30-day window.
Chapter 6

Network and Observability

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the Microsoft.Network model for the Container Apps Environment in DEV (open) versus STAGE/PROD (VNet-integrated, private endpoints).
  • Maps Azure Monitor / Log Analytics / Application Insights flow with API versions and KQL examples.
  • Frames Microsoft Sentinel as the audit-and-detection overlay on the Layer 1 workspace.
60-second read
  • DEV deploys Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments without VNet; STAGE/PROD add VNet integration · private endpoints to ACR/Key Vault/Storage · private DNS · NSGs.
  • Azure Monitor pipelines: app logs to Microsoft.OperationalInsights · traces/metrics to Microsoft.Insights via OpenTelemetry — no custom configuration in agent code.
  • The LEDGER mirrors to Application Insights as custom events so Microsoft Sentinel can correlate audit decisions with platform events.
  • Microsoft Sentinel and Microsoft Purview attach to Layer 1's Log Analytics workspace; both licensed separately and toggled per tenant in the Day-Zero checklist.

6.1 The DEV-Tier Network Posture

For Lab v0.1 (DEV), Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments deploys without VNet integration. Ingress on every Container App is set to internal — accessible only from inside the environment's auto-assigned address space — and the harness reaches agents through the environment's HTTP routing. Public access is closed by default; the only public surface is the Application Insights query endpoint (used by delivery leads investigating telemetry).

This is sufficient for Lab v0.1 because Lab fixtures are synthetic (no client data). It is not sufficient for STAGE or PROD.

6.2 The STAGE / PROD Network Posture

STAGE and PROD add VNet integration and private endpoints. Concrete additions:

Microsoft resource typeAPI versionPurpose
Microsoft.Network/virtualNetworks2023-09-01Tenant VNet hosting the Container Apps environment subnet
Microsoft.Network/virtualNetworks/subnets2023-09-01Delegated subnet for Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments (≥ /23 required)
Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints2023-09-01Private endpoints for container registry, Key Vault, Storage, Cosmos DB, Azure SQL
Microsoft.Network/privateDnsZones2020-06-01Per-service private DNS (privatelink.azurecr.io, privatelink.vaultcore.azure.net, etc.)
Microsoft.Network/networkSecurityGroups2023-09-01NSG on Container Apps subnet — egress to Microsoft platform endpoints + audit-row destinations only

The Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments resource gains a vnetConfiguration block that points at the delegated subnet:

BICEP — managedEnvironments STAGE/PROD overlay
vnetConfiguration: {
  infrastructureSubnetId: subnet.id
  internal: true
  platformReservedCidr: '10.244.0.0/16'
  platformReservedDnsIP: '10.244.0.2'
}
Note — Subnet sizing

Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments requires a delegated subnet of at least /23 for the Consumption profile and /27 per Dedicated workload profile. Plan the VNet allocation at PROD before STAGE is too small to upgrade in place.

6.3 Azure Monitor — The Telemetry Pipeline

Every Container App in every Service in every tenant emits telemetry via two Microsoft-native pipelines:

  1. App logs to Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces via the appLogsConfiguration on the managed environment. Tables: ContainerAppConsoleLogs_CL, ContainerAppSystemLogs_CL.
  2. Application traces / metrics to Microsoft.Insights/components via the APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING env var auto-injected by Container Apps when daprAIConnectionString is set on the environment.

The agent code does not configure these. The opentelemetry-instrumentation-fastapi and azure-monitor-opentelemetry packages, when imported, auto-detect the connection string and stream traces / metrics to Application Insights, which writes through to Log Analytics.

6.3.1 The audit-row mirror in Log Analytics

Every LEDGER row written by an agent is mirrored as an Application Insights custom event with the row hash and key fields. This mirror is a secondary view; the LEDGER table itself remains the source of truth. The mirror exists so that Microsoft Sentinel rules can correlate LEDGER decisions with platform-level events (Microsoft.KeyVault unwraps, Microsoft.ContainerRegistry pulls, Microsoft Entra ID sign-ins) without joining across stores.

KQL — example correlation query
customEvents
| where name == "ledger.row.committed"
| where customDimensions.agent_id == "the-analyst"
| project ts=timestamp,
          run_id=customDimensions.run_id,
          step_id=customDimensions.step_id,
          row_hash=customDimensions._hash,
          cost=todouble(customDimensions.cost_per_decision),
          safety=customDimensions.safety_verdict
| where ts > ago(1h)
| order by ts desc

6.4 Microsoft Sentinel — The Detection Overlay

Microsoft Sentinel attaches to the Layer 1 Log Analytics workspace by enabling the Sentinel solution. Sentinel adds:

  • Analytics rules — KQL-based detections on the LEDGER mirror table. Anomalous unwrap rates, repeated verify_chain failures, unusual cost-per-decision spikes.
  • UEBA — User and Entity Behavior Analytics, applied to the four user-assigned managed identities. Detects cross-Service identity reuse anomalies.
  • SOAR playbooks — Logic Apps that auto-respond (suspend Container App revision, rotate Key Vault secret, page on-call) when an analytics rule fires.

Sentinel is licensed separately from Layer 1; the Bicep does not deploy it. Day-Zero onboarding (Appendix B) records whether Sentinel is in scope for this client tenant; if so, the Sentinel workspace solution is enabled before STAGE-tier deployment.

6.5 Microsoft Purview — Catalog and Lineage

Microsoft Purview is the catalog layer; it appears in deployment.md but is largely invisible to the Layer 1 Bicep. Purview connects to:

  • Microsoft Fabric Lakehouses where bronze/silver/gold land — automatic catalog scan.
  • The LEDGER table — exposed as a Purview asset with custom classifications for audit-row fields.
  • Microsoft.KeyVault — keys cataloged with sensitivity classification (CMK, PII unwrap key, etc.).

Purview is a Layer 1 concern in the sense that it lives at tenant scope; but its registration of Layer 2 / Layer 3 assets happens as those layers deploy. Day-Zero records the Purview account ID; per-Service onboarding (Appendix C) registers the new Service's data sources.

Key Takeaways

  • DEV deploys Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments without VNet; STAGE/PROD add VNet integration, private endpoints to container registry/Key Vault/Storage, private DNS, and NSGs.
  • Azure Monitor pipelines: app logs to Microsoft.OperationalInsights, traces/metrics to Microsoft.Insights via OpenTelemetry — no custom configuration in agent code.
  • The LEDGER mirrors to Application Insights as custom events so Microsoft Sentinel can correlate audit decisions with platform events.
  • Microsoft Sentinel and Microsoft Purview attach to Layer 1's Log Analytics workspace; both are licensed separately and toggled per tenant in the Day-Zero checklist.

Deployment Drills

  1. Run the KQL audit-correlation query in §6.3.1 against your Lab Application Insights. Confirm customEvents returns LEDGER rows.
  2. For each Microsoft.Network resource in §6.2, confirm the Bicep parameterizes the address space and does not hardcode CIDRs.
  3. If your tenant has Microsoft Sentinel licensed, enable the solution on the Layer 1 workspace and import the starter analytics rule pack for Container Apps.
Chapter 6A

Deploying Microsoft Fabric

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the deployment of every Fabric primitive APEX-M depends on — capacity · workspaces · domains · Lakehouses · Eventhouses · Mirrored databases · semantic models — with Bicep and CLI/REST where Bicep is not yet available.
  • Maps the SKU envelope per APEX environment tier (Lab v0.1 / DEV / STAGE / PROD) and the cost profile for each.
  • Specifies identity, network isolation, deployment-pipeline mechanics, and capacity-management knobs (bursting · smoothing · surge protection · pause/resume).
  • Establishes the Lab-to-PROD promotion model that pairs Fabric deployment pipelines with Git integration and the four-stamp audit anchor.
90-second read
  • Capacity is Bicep. Microsoft.Fabric/capacities@2023-11-01 (stable · production default per Microsoft Learn and AVM avm/res/fabric/capacity) — name · location · F SKU · admin members. Use @2025-01-15-preview only if you require preview-only features and your Azure Policy allows preview API pins.
  • Workspaces and items are not Bicep (yet). Provision via the Fabric REST API (api.fabric.microsoft.com), the fab CLI, or the Fabric portal. APEX-M wraps these in deployment-scripts/fabric-workspaces.ps1 invoked by the Layer 1 Bicep.
  • Two deployment models: per-Service capacity (one F-SKU per Service · maximum isolation · highest cost) vs per-practice capacity (one F-SKU per practice · workspaces share the capacity · APEX-M default).
  • Per-environment SKU envelope: Lab v0.1 = F2 (paused outside hours) · DEV = F8 · STAGE = F32 · PROD = F64+ (F64 is the Power BI Viewer-free threshold).
  • RTI & Mirroring are workspace-item-level — created via REST after the workspace exists. Eventhouse + KQL Database + Eventstream + Activator + Mirrored Database are all REST-driven; APEX-M ships deployment-scripts/rti-bootstrap.ps1 per Service.
  • Identity: Fabric-native four roles (Admin · Member · Contributor · Viewer); per-item OneLake security roles for granular reads (Viewer-only); APEX agent identities (Foundry-issued) granted Viewer + OneLake security policy.
  • Promotion via deployment pipelines: Git integration on each Service workspace → 3-stage pipeline (DEV → STAGE → PROD). Semantic models, notebooks, pipelines, reports promote together; Lakehouse data does not (each environment has its own).

6A.1 Two Deployment Models

APEX-M supports two production capacity-allocation models:

Per-Service capacityPer-practice capacity (APEX-M default)
Capacities per practiceOne per Service (e.g., five F32 for the five RC Services)One per practice (e.g., one F128 for all five RC Services)
Throttling blast radiusService-isolatedService-shared within a practice; practice-isolated across practices
CostHigher — sum of per-Service SKUsLower — F128 ≈ 4 × F32 in CUs but at lower price-per-CU
Operational simplicityComplex — per-Service scaling, billing, monitoringSimpler — one knob per practice
When to chooseService has SLA-distinct latency requirements (e.g., HLS-E2E-04 prior-auth needs sub-second; HLS-E2E-02 claims-denial tolerates seconds)Default for most engagements; Services within a practice typically share workload-shape characteristics

The Bicep blueprint infra/bicep/blueprints/w1-foundation.bicep exposes both via a parameter capacityAllocationModel with values per-service and per-practice.

6A.2 Bicep — Microsoft.Fabric/capacities

Per the Microsoft.Fabric capacities resource definition (stable API version 2023-11-01), the capacity is a small Bicep resource. APEX-M production templates pin to the stable API version; the preview 2025-01-15-preview is reserved for tenants that have opted into preview-only Fabric capacity features.

infra/bicep/platform/fabric.bicep · per-practice F128 PROD capacity
@description('Practice code, e.g., rc, hls, er, axle, th, tmt, ice')
param practiceCode string

@description('Environment tier: lab | dev | stage | prod')
@allowed(['lab', 'dev', 'stage', 'prod'])
param tier string

@description('Microsoft Entra ID object IDs of the Fabric capacity admins (Service Principal or group)')
param adminMembers array

@description('Azure region — must match the rest of the APEX-M platform region')
param location string = resourceGroup().location

var skuMap = {
  lab: 'F2'
  dev: 'F8'
  stage: 'F32'
  prod: 'F128'   // override per practice if needed
}

resource fabricCapacity 'Microsoft.Fabric/capacities@2023-11-01' = {
  name: 'cap-apex-${practiceCode}-${tier}'
  location: location
  sku: {
    name: skuMap[tier]
    tier: 'Fabric'
  }
  properties: {
    administration: {
      members: adminMembers
    }
  }
  tags: {
    'apex.tier': tier
    'apex.practice': practiceCode
    'apex.workload': 'data-plane'
  }
}

output capacityId string = fabricCapacity.id
output capacityName string = fabricCapacity.name

Microsoft also publishes an Azure Verified Module (avm/res/fabric/capacity) that wraps this resource with WAF-aligned defaults · diagnostic settings · locks · role assignments. APEX-M's blueprint uses the AVM module for STAGE/PROD and the raw resource for Lab/DEV.

6A.3 Workspaces & Items — REST/CLI Provisioning

As of this writing, Fabric workspace and item provisioning is not ARM/Bicep-native. APEX-M wraps Fabric REST + the fab CLI in deployment scripts invoked from the Layer 1 Bicep:

deployment-scripts/fabric-workspaces.ps1 · APEX-M canonical wrapper
param(
  [string]$CapacityName,
  [string]$ServiceCode,        # e.g., rc-e2e-03
  [string]$Tier,               # dev | stage | prod
  [string]$DomainName          # e.g., apex-rc
)

# 1. Authenticate as the deploying SP (managed identity in CI / azd)
Connect-AzAccount -Identity

# 2. Get tokens for Fabric API + Power BI API
$fabricToken = (Get-AzAccessToken -ResourceUrl 'https://api.fabric.microsoft.com').Token

# 3. Create the workspace if missing
$workspaceName = "apex-${ServiceCode}-${Tier}"
$body = @{
  displayName = $workspaceName
  capacityId = (Get-AzResource -Name $CapacityName -ResourceType 'Microsoft.Fabric/capacities').Properties.capacityObjectId
  description = "APEX-M Service workspace for ${ServiceCode} in ${Tier}"
} | ConvertTo-Json
Invoke-RestMethod -Method POST -Uri 'https://api.fabric.microsoft.com/v1/workspaces' `
  -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $fabricToken" } -Body $body -ContentType 'application/json'

# 4. Assign workspace to the practice domain
$domainId = (Get-FabricDomain -Name $DomainName).Id
Set-FabricWorkspaceDomain -WorkspaceName $workspaceName -DomainId $domainId

# 5. Create the standard items: bronze + silver Lakehouse, Gold Warehouse, Eventhouse
foreach ($item in @(
  @{ type='Lakehouse'; name='bronze' },
  @{ type='Lakehouse'; name='silver' },
  @{ type='Warehouse'; name='gold' },
  @{ type='Eventhouse'; name='rti' }
)) {
  New-FabricItem -WorkspaceName $workspaceName -Type $item.type -DisplayName $item.name
}
Note — Bicep coverage growing

Fabric API surface area in Bicep/ARM is expanding. Track the change log for new resource types beyond capacities. As workspace and item resources become Bicep-native, the script wrapper above collapses into a Bicep module. The contract is unchanged: per-Service workspace, standard item set, Git integration on the workspace.

6A.4 SKU Selection per Tier — The Cost Envelope

Pricing scales linearly with CUs but not with SKU count. APEX-M's reference cost envelope (US East 2 list price; replace with engagement region):

TierSKUCUsApprox hourly costPause when idle?Annualised (24×7 PROD)
Laptop——$0—$0
Lab v0.1F22~$0.36/hrYes — paused outside business hours via Bicep deployment script~$3,150 if always-on (paused: ~$1,000)
DEVF88~$1.45/hrYesPaused: ~$4,000
STAGEF3232~$5.78/hrOff-hours pause optional~$50,600
PROD (per practice, F128)F128128~$23.10/hrNever~$202,000
PROD (large, F256)F256256~$46.20/hrNever~$405,000

OneLake storage is metered separately — pay for bytes stored, no charge for transactions (transactions consume CUs). For a typical APEX-M Service with 6 months of Bronze + Silver + Gold retention, OneLake storage runs in the low-thousands annual.

Best Practice — pause non-PROD aggressively

Fabric capacities billed per second when running. Lab and DEV capacities should be paused on weekends and outside business hours. APEX-M ships deployment-scripts/fabric-pause-schedule.ps1 registered as a daily Logic Apps trigger; ROI is roughly 60% of the non-PROD Fabric line item.

6A.5 Identity, RBAC, and OneLake Security

Three identity surfaces compose:

SurfaceMechanismAPEX-M assignment
Capacity adminMicrosoft Entra ID identity with capacity-level rights (settings · pause · resume · workspace assignment)Engagement lead's PAW account · break-glass identity (per Sellers Guide Appendix E)
Workspace roles (4 roles)Admin · Member · Contributor · ViewerService architects = Admin · Engineers = Member · BU SMEs = Contributor · Operators (read dashboards) = Viewer · agents = Viewer with OneLake security policy
OneLake security (preview)Granular policies — table · folder · column · row level. Apply only to Viewers (Admin/Member/Contributor bypass).Agent identity assigned Viewer role · per-Service OneLake security policy restricts reads to Service-owned tables and Service-relevant rows (e.g., RC-E2E-03 agent reads only stores in its scope)

Bicep grants the platform identity (Layer 1) capacity-admin rights; the fabric-workspaces.ps1 deployment script grants per-Service workspace roles after workspace creation. Per-agent OneLake security policies are authored in YAML and applied via deployment-scripts/onelake-security.ps1 after agent identities are issued by Foundry (per Ch 11.5.6).

6A.6 Domains — Per-Practice Grouping

Per Services Guide §1.5.11, APEX-M groups Service workspaces into domains by practice. Domain creation is REST-only:

deployment-scripts/fabric-domains.ps1 · per-practice domain bootstrap
$practices = @('rc', 'hls', 'er', 'axle', 'th', 'tmt', 'ice', 'shared')

foreach ($p in $practices) {
  $body = @{
    displayName = "apex-${p}"
    description = "APEX domain for the ${p.ToUpper()} practice"
  } | ConvertTo-Json
  Invoke-RestMethod -Method POST `
    -Uri 'https://api.fabric.microsoft.com/v1/admin/domains' `
    -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $fabricToken" } `
    -Body $body -ContentType 'application/json'
}

Domain admins are delegated per-practice; the apex-shared domain is owned by the platform team and contains the canonical-schema Lakehouses (per Services Guide §1.5.13).

6A.7 Real-Time Intelligence — Eventhouse · Eventstream · Activator

RTI items are workspace-scoped and provisioned via REST or the fab CLI:

deployment-scripts/rti-bootstrap.ps1 · per-Service RTI fleet
param([string]$WorkspaceId, [string]$ServiceCode)

# 1. Create the Eventhouse (auto-creates default KQL Database)
$eh = New-FabricItem -WorkspaceId $WorkspaceId -Type 'Eventhouse' -DisplayName "${ServiceCode}-rti"

# 2. Create additional KQL Databases (one per data class)
foreach ($db in @('events', 'telemetry', 'audit')) {
  New-FabricKqlDatabase -EventhouseId $eh.Id -DisplayName "${ServiceCode}-${db}"
}

# 3. Create the Eventstream
$es = New-FabricItem -WorkspaceId $WorkspaceId -Type 'Eventstream' `
  -DisplayName "${ServiceCode}-eventstream"

# 4. Add a source (e.g., Event Hubs) and a destination (Eventhouse + Activator)
Add-FabricEventstreamSource -EventstreamId $es.Id -Type 'EventHubs' `
  -ConnectionString '@Microsoft.KeyVault(SecretUri=...)'
Add-FabricEventstreamDestination -EventstreamId $es.Id -Type 'Eventhouse' `
  -EventhouseId $eh.Id -KqlDatabaseName "${ServiceCode}-events"

# 5. Create an Activator with a KQL queryset alert
$act = New-FabricItem -WorkspaceId $WorkspaceId -Type 'Reflex' `
  -DisplayName "${ServiceCode}-activator"
New-FabricActivatorRule -ActivatorId $act.Id `
  -KqlQuery "Excursions | where Severity > 0.7 | project StoreId, SkuId, Severity" `
  -Action 'POST https://logic-apps-host/api/excursion-trigger'

The output is a turnkey RTI fleet: events flow Eventstream → Eventhouse · Activator subscribes to a KQL query · APEX agent fleet receives the trigger via Logic Apps.

6A.8 Mirroring — SOR Bronze Setup

Mirroring is provisioned per-source via REST, with the source connection authenticated through Microsoft Entra-ID-issued credentials (managed identity is preferred per the April 2026 Eventstream support note):

deployment-scripts/fabric-mirror.ps1 · D365 mirroring example
param(
  [string]$WorkspaceId,
  [string]$ServiceCode,
  [string]$D365Environment,            # e.g., contoso-prod.crm.dynamics.com
  [string]$ManagedIdentityResourceId   # APEX-M ingest UAMI
)

$body = @{
  displayName = "${ServiceCode}-bronze-d365"
  type = 'MirroredDatabase'
  source = @{
    type = 'Dynamics365'
    environment = $D365Environment
    authentication = @{
      type = 'ManagedIdentity'
      resourceId = $ManagedIdentityResourceId
    }
    tables = @('account', 'contact', 'incident', 'msdyn_workorder')   # per Service
  }
  destination = @{
    workspaceId = $WorkspaceId
    lakehouseName = 'bronze'
  }
} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6

Invoke-RestMethod -Method POST `
  -Uri "https://api.fabric.microsoft.com/v1/workspaces/${WorkspaceId}/mirroredDatabases" `
  -Headers @{ Authorization = "Bearer $fabricToken" } `
  -Body $body -ContentType 'application/json'

For sources without a built-in connector, APEX-M uses open mirroring — a designated OneLake folder receives Delta Parquet writes from the source, and Fabric handles the rest. The provider (the SOR or an integration partner) writes; APEX consumes.

6A.9 Direct Lake Semantic Model Deployment

Semantic models are Power BI items in the workspace. APEX-M authors them as Power BI Project (PBIP) files in Git and deploys via Git integration; the deployment-pipeline mechanic moves them across DEV → STAGE → PROD.

apps/services/rc-e2e-03/semantic-model/definition.pbism · Direct Lake on OneLake
{
  "name": "rc-e2e-03-direct-lake",
  "compatibilityLevel": 1604,
  "model": {
    "annotations": [
      {
        "name": "PBIDesktopVersion",
        "value": "2.130.0+"
      }
    ],
    "tables": [
      // Tables loaded from OneLake via Direct Lake on OneLake mode
      // Source expressions reference: AzureStorage.DataLake(
      //   "https://onelake.dfs.fabric.microsoft.com/{wsId}/{lhId}"
      // )
    ]
  }
}

The PBIP file is deployed into the workspace via Git integration; Power BI Desktop's live edit mode (per Direct Lake in Power BI Desktop) syncs changes back to the workspace. APEX-M workflows author in DEV, validate in STAGE, promote to PROD via the Fabric deployment pipeline.

6A.10 Network Isolation — Tenant Settings & Private Links

Three tenant-level Fabric settings APEX-M requires for STAGE/PROD:

SettingConfigurationWhy
Private Link for tenantEnabled · per-region private endpointAll Fabric web traffic transits Microsoft Private Link · no public-internet exposure of workspace UIs or APIs
Block public Internet accessEnabled (PROD) · audit-only (STAGE)Blocks tenants public Internet path even with Private Link enabled — defense in depth
Workspace IP firewall (preview)Enabled per workspace · CIDR allowlist for the apex-m platform VNetWorkspace-scoped firewall (referenced in Ch 17 §17.4 of this book) — narrowest blast radius
Managed private endpoints (Fabric → external)Configured per Mirrored DatabaseFabric-managed VNet integration so Mirroring can reach private SORs (Azure SQL with private endpoint, on-prem via gateway)
On-premises data gatewayRequired when SOR is on-premises and not internet-reachableTunnels Mirroring traffic from Fabric to on-prem SOR via the gateway

6A.11 Capacity Management — Bursting · Smoothing · Surge · Pause

APEX-M's PROD capacity tuning checklist:

deployment-scripts/capacity-tune-prod.ps1 · PROD capacity hardening
param([string]$CapacityName)

# 1. Enable surge protection (cap background-job consumption to 70% of CUs)
Set-FabricCapacitySurgeProtection -Name $CapacityName -BackgroundJobsCap 70

# 2. Enable disaster recovery (per Microsoft.Fabric reliability guide)
Set-FabricCapacityDisasterRecovery -Name $CapacityName -Enabled $true

# 3. Configure capacity-usage notifications (email at 75% / 90% / 100%)
Set-FabricCapacityNotification -Name $CapacityName `
  -Email 'apex-m-ops@contoso.com' `
  -Thresholds @(75, 90, 100)

# 4. Designate as Copilot capacity (if the engagement uses Fabric Copilot)
Set-FabricCapacityCopilotEnabled -Name $CapacityName -Enabled $true

# 5. Configure Power BI workload settings (semantic model size limits, AI quotas)
Set-FabricCapacityPowerBIWorkload -Name $CapacityName `
  -SemanticModelMaxSizeGB 10 `
  -PaginatedReportEnabled $true `
  -AIWorkloadEnabled $true

6A.12 Promotion — Git Integration + Deployment Pipelines

APEX-M's promotion model pairs Fabric's two CI/CD primitives:

  • Git integration on every Service workspace. Each workspace is bound to a branch in the Service's Azure DevOps / GitHub repo. Notebooks · pipelines · semantic models · reports · KQL querysets serialise to the repo as YAML/JSON. PRs against the branch are reviewed before merge.
  • Deployment pipelines for cross-stage promotion. A 3-stage pipeline (DEV → STAGE → PROD) promotes items between the per-stage workspaces. Lakehouse data does not promote — each stage has its own; only the definitions (notebook code, pipeline definitions, semantic model schema) move.
deployment-scripts/fabric-pipeline.ps1 · 3-stage pipeline per Service
param([string]$ServiceCode)

# Create the pipeline
$pipeline = New-FabricDeploymentPipeline `
  -DisplayName "apex-${ServiceCode}-pipeline" `
  -Description "DEV → STAGE → PROD for ${ServiceCode}"

# Bind workspaces to stages
Set-FabricPipelineStage -PipelineId $pipeline.Id -Stage 'Development' `
  -WorkspaceName "apex-${ServiceCode}-dev"
Set-FabricPipelineStage -PipelineId $pipeline.Id -Stage 'Test' `
  -WorkspaceName "apex-${ServiceCode}-stage"
Set-FabricPipelineStage -PipelineId $pipeline.Id -Stage 'Production' `
  -WorkspaceName "apex-${ServiceCode}-prod"

# Configure deployment rules (per-stage parameter overrides)
# e.g., point Lakehouse references to per-stage Lakehouse IDs
Add-FabricPipelineDeploymentRule -PipelineId $pipeline.Id -Stage 'Production' `
  -ItemType 'SemanticModel' -ItemName 'rc-e2e-03-direct-lake' `
  -RuleType 'DataSourceRule' `
  -OverrideValue 'https://onelake.dfs.fabric.microsoft.com/{prodWsId}/{lakehouseId}'

Per Services Guide §1.5.13, the per-Service pipeline is the unit APEX-M promotes. Combined with image-SHA promotion for the agent fleet (Ch 12 of this book) and Bicep promotion for the Layer 1 platform, the full APEX-M deploy is three coordinated promotions: Bicep → Fabric pipeline → image SHA. The four-stamp audit anchor (manifest · policy · prompt · agent-version) carries through all three.

6A.13 Cost Management — CU Budgeting per Service

The Cost envelope (per Services Guide §30.1.6) requires per-Service CU attribution. Fabric makes this possible via two mechanisms:

  • Tags on the capacity Bicep resource — propagate to Microsoft Cost Management for capacity-level cost. Per-practice tagging gives practice-level rollup.
  • Fabric Capacity Metrics App — per-workspace and per-item CU consumption · throttling events · queue length. APEX-M exports daily snapshots to Microsoft.OperationalInsights for Power BI Direct Lake reporting alongside agent cost-per-decision (§29.5).

The Cost envelope budget per Service is set in the Service Bicep parameters (monthlyCuBudget); the daily snapshot job emits a LEDGER row tagging burn-rate alerts when the envelope exceeds 80% mid-month.

Key Takeaways

  • Capacity is Bicep (Microsoft.Fabric/capacities@2023-11-01 stable · AVM module preferred); workspaces and items are REST/CLI driven via deployment-scripts/fabric-*.ps1 wrappers.
  • APEX-M default capacity model is per-practice: one F-SKU per practice hosts all that practice's Service workspaces; PROD entry SKU is F128 (or F64 for small tenants where Power BI Viewer-free matters).
  • SKU envelope: Lab F2 (paused) · DEV F8 · STAGE F32 · PROD F64–F2048. Pause non-PROD outside business hours via scheduled Logic Apps.
  • RTI (Eventhouse · Eventstream · Activator) and Mirroring are workspace-item-level; provisioned via REST after the workspace exists. APEX-M ships rti-bootstrap.ps1 + fabric-mirror.ps1 per Service.
  • Identity layers compose: Capacity admin · Workspace 4 roles · OneLake security policies (Viewer-only granular reads). Agents are Viewer + per-Service OneLake security policy.
  • Promotion uses Git integration (per workspace) plus deployment pipelines (DEV → STAGE → PROD); definitions promote, data does not.
  • Cost envelope per Service is enforced by capacity tagging + Fabric Capacity Metrics App snapshots into LEDGER (burn-rate alerts at 80% of envelope).

Deployment Drills

  1. Write the Bicep for one practice's PROD capacity (per-practice model, F128). Tag it with the engagement code, practice, tier. Validate azd provision creates it cleanly.
  2. Run fabric-workspaces.ps1 for one Service in DEV. Verify the workspace, the four standard items (Lakehouse bronze · silver · Warehouse gold · Eventhouse rti), and the domain assignment.
  3. Configure a deployment pipeline for one Service. Promote a notebook change DEV → STAGE; verify the data-source rule rewrites Lakehouse references; confirm STAGE notebook reads STAGE Lakehouse.
  4. Inspect your Fabric Capacity Metrics App for one PROD capacity. Identify which Service consumes the most CUs in a typical day. Set capacity-usage notifications and surge-protection ceiling.
  5. Pick one client SOR. Decide between built-in Mirroring · open mirroring · Data Factory pipeline. Write the deployment-script invocation for whichever you chose.
Chapter 6B

Deploying Microsoft Sentinel & Defender

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the Bicep deployment of Microsoft Sentinel (the Microsoft.OperationalInsights / Microsoft.SecurityInsights pair) and the seven canonical APEX-M detection rules.
  • Configures Defender for Cloud · Defender XDR · Defender for Cloud Apps coverage on the APEX-M subscription.
  • Deploys SOAR playbooks (Logic Apps) wired to detections.
  • Provisions Microsoft Security Copilot capacity where the engagement licences it.
60-second read
  • Sentinel = Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces (Log Analytics) + Microsoft.SecurityInsights/onboardingStates (Sentinel-on). Same workspace as APEX-M telemetry.
  • Detections deploy as Microsoft.SecurityInsights/alertRules · seven APEX-M rules per Service per §32A.2.
  • Defender for Cloud enabled at subscription level via Microsoft.Security/pricings; one resource per Defender plan (Servers · Containers · KeyVault · Storage · CosmosDB · DNS).
  • Defender XDR activated via Microsoft 365 admin · Defender XDR portal correlates Sentinel + endpoint + identity signals.
  • Playbooks are Logic Apps Standard · wired to Sentinel automation rules · invoked on alert with full context payload.
  • Microsoft Security Copilot capacity is Microsoft.SecurityCopilot/capacities (preview); APEX-M provisions when engagement budget supports.

6B.1 Sentinel-on-Log-Analytics Bicep

infra/bicep/platform/sentinel.bicep
@description('Engagement code')
param engagementCode string
@description('Tier')
param tier string
param location string = resourceGroup().location

resource workspace 'Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces@2023-09-01' = {
  name: 'log-${engagementCode}-${tier}'
  location: location
  properties: {
    sku: { name: 'PerGB2018' }
    retentionInDays: tier == 'prod' ? 365 : 90
    features: { enableLogAccessUsingOnlyResourcePermissions: true }
  }
}

resource sentinelOnboarding 'Microsoft.SecurityInsights/onboardingStates@2023-11-01' = {
  scope: workspace
  name: 'default'
  properties: {}
}

resource ueba 'Microsoft.SecurityInsights/settings@2023-11-01' = {
  scope: workspace
  name: 'Ueba'
  kind: 'Ueba'
  properties: { dataSources: ['AuditLogs', 'AzureActivity', 'SigninLogs'] }
}

output workspaceId string = workspace.id
output workspaceCustomerId string = workspace.properties.customerId

6B.2 Detection Rules Bicep

infra/bicep/platform/sentinel-detections.bicep · seven core APEX-M rules
@description('Sentinel/Log Analytics workspace name')
param workspaceName string
param serviceCode string

resource workspace 'Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces@2023-09-01' existing = {
  name: workspaceName
}

// Detection 1 · agent identity high-risk
resource det001 'Microsoft.SecurityInsights/alertRules@2023-11-01' = {
  scope: workspace
  name: guid(workspaceName, serviceCode, 'det-001')
  kind: 'Scheduled'
  properties: {
    displayName: 'APEX-M-DET-001 ${serviceCode} agent identity high risk'
    severity: 'High'
    enabled: true
    query: loadTextContent('detections/${serviceCode}-det-001.kql')
    queryFrequency: 'PT15M'
    queryPeriod: 'PT1H'
    triggerOperator: 'GreaterThan'
    triggerThreshold: 0
    suppressionEnabled: false
    incidentConfiguration: { createIncident: true }
  }
}

// Repeat for det-002 through det-007 with corresponding KQL files;
// APEX-M ships per-Service templated detections under
//   infra/bicep/platform/detections/<service>-det-XXX.kql

6B.4 SOAR Playbook Bicep · Auto-Disable Risky Agent

infra/bicep/platform/playbook-disable-agent.bicep
resource playbook 'Microsoft.Logic/workflows@2019-05-01' = {
  name: 'pb-${engagementCode}-disable-risky-agent'
  location: location
  identity: { type: 'SystemAssigned' }
  properties: {
    state: 'Enabled'
    definition: {
      '$schema': 'https://schema.management.azure.com/providers/Microsoft.Logic/schemas/2016-06-01/workflowdefinition.json#'
      triggers: {
        SentinelAlertTrigger: {
          type: 'ApiConnectionWebhook'
          inputs: {
            host: { connection: { name: '@parameters(\'$connections\')[\'azuresentinel\'][\'connectionId\']' } }
            body: { callback_url: '@{listCallbackUrl()}' }
            path: '/subscribe'
          }
        }
      }
      actions: {
        ParseAlert: { type: 'ParseJson' /* ... */ }
        DisableAgentIdentity: {
          type: 'Http'
          inputs: {
            method: 'PATCH'
            uri: 'https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/servicePrincipals/@{body(\'ParseAlert\')[\'agentObjectId\']}'
            authentication: { type: 'ManagedServiceIdentity' }
            body: { accountEnabled: false }
          }
        }
        PageOnCall: {
          type: 'Http'
          inputs: { method: 'POST'; uri: '@{parameters(\'pagerDutyWebhook\')}'; body: { /* ... */ } }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

// Sentinel automation rule wires the playbook to detection 1
resource automationRule 'Microsoft.SecurityInsights/automationRules@2023-11-01' = {
  scope: workspace
  name: guid(workspaceName, 'auto-rule-001')
  properties: {
    displayName: 'auto-disable-on-DET-001'
    triggeringLogic: {
      isEnabled: true
      triggersOn: 'Alerts'
      conditions: [{ /* match displayName begins with APEX-M-DET-001 */ }]
    }
    actions: [{
      actionType: 'RunPlaybook'
      actionConfiguration: { logicAppResourceId: playbook.id }
    }]
  }
}

6B.5 Defender XDR Activation

Defender XDR is activated through the Microsoft 365 admin portal (no Bicep). One-time per tenant:

  1. Navigate to https://security.microsoft.com
  2. Onboard subscription · accept data processing terms
  3. Configure data connectors · turn on Microsoft Sentinel integration (uses workspaces from §6B.1)
  4. Configure custom detection rules using Defender XDR's advanced hunting (KQL · references the same data tables as Sentinel)
  5. Enable the agentic AI signal set. In Defender XDR, turn on the AI workloads connector and the AI.Azure_Agentic_* alert family. Confirm the AIAgentsInfo table is populating in the Layer 1 Log Analytics workspace.

6B.5.1 AIAgentsInfo · The Agent-Shaped Pivot Table

The Sentinel + Defender stack now exposes AIAgentsInfo — a dedicated table inventorying every AI agent the tenant operates. APEX-M Sentinel detection rules join against it to enrich each alert with the named agent, its blueprint, and its Service code:

KQL · enrich a detection with AIAgentsInfo
SigninLogs
| where ResourceDisplayName == "Microsoft Azure AI Foundry"
| where RiskLevel == "high"
| join kind=leftouter (
    AIAgentsInfo
    | project AgentId, AgentName, BlueprintName, ServiceCode = tostring(Tags.apex_service_code)
  ) on AgentId
| project TimeGenerated, AgentName, ServiceCode, BlueprintName, RiskState, RiskEventTypes_V2

6B.5.2 Defender AI.Azure_Agentic_* Alerts

The new Defender alert family covers agent-specific threat patterns. APEX-M maps each to a SOAR playbook from §6B.4:

AlertWhat it catchesAPEX-M playbook
AI.Azure_Agentic_AnomalousToolCallAgent invokes a tool outside its declared MCP catalogPause agent version · open incident · 30-min SLA
AI.Azure_Agentic_PromptInjectionSignatureContent-Safety prompt-shield "indirect" match in a tool result the agent then consumedQuarantine the upstream tool result · invalidate any decisions taken on it in the last 15 minutes
AI.Azure_Agentic_IdentityAbuseAgent identity sign-in pattern matches abuse signature from Microsoft Threat IntelligenceAuto-disable agent identity · freeze blueprint · engagement-lead-paged incident
AI.Azure_Agentic_TaskAdherenceDriftContent Safety Task Adherence (§14C.2) score collapsed for an agent over a rolling windowRoll back agent version to previous · pause promotion · service architect investigates

6B.5.3 Security Copilot Dynamic Threat Detection Agent

Microsoft Security Copilot ships a Dynamic Threat Detection Agent (DTDA) that authors and tunes Sentinel detections from your environment's actual telemetry. APEX-M's recommended workflow:

  1. Engagement security lead points DTDA at the Layer 1 Sentinel workspace and at ApexLedger_CL / AIAgentsInfo / per-Service Application Insights.
  2. DTDA generates candidate detection rules. Each candidate includes the rule body, a sample-data justification, and a false-positive-rate estimate.
  3. The engagement security lead reviews, edits, and approves. Approved rules deploy via the same infra/bicep/platform/sentinel-rules.bicep as the seven core rules (§6B.2) so they version-control alongside the rest.

DTDA's own actions land in AIAgentsInfo and are audited per §14C — agents writing detections about agents is recursive on purpose, with human approval as the stopping condition.

6B.5.4 Sentinel Custom Connector Builder + Sentinel MCP Server

Two additional Sentinel surfaces APEX-M uses:

  • Sentinel Custom Connector Builder agent. Builds custom data connectors for APEX-specific telemetry (LEDGER · per-Service eval results · model card metadata) without hand-coding. Replaces the deployment-scripts/sentinel-custom-connector.ps1 hand-rolled connector that earlier APEX-M drafts shipped.
  • Sentinel MCP server. Sentinel exposes an MCP server — AI agents can query KQL, fetch incident metadata, and run hunting queries as MCP tool calls. APEX-M's MCP catalog registers it under microsoft.sentinel so SOC-facing agents (TH replay, IRM enrichment) can call it directly.

6B.6 Microsoft Security Copilot · Optional Capacity

infra/bicep/platform/security-copilot.bicep · optional
resource secCopilotCapacity 'Microsoft.SecurityCopilot/capacities@2024-11-01-preview' = {

  properties: {

    crossGeoCompute: 'NotAllowed'           // PROD per region; explicit opt-in
    crossTenantCompute: 'NotAllowed'        // ADR-025-01 binding
  }
}

Key Takeaways

  • Sentinel deploys as Log Analytics workspace + Microsoft.SecurityInsights onboarding. Same workspace as APEX telemetry.
  • Seven canonical detection rules deploy as Microsoft.SecurityInsights/alertRules with KQL loaded from per-Service template files.
  • Defender for Cloud plans deploy at subscription scope; Defender for AI Services + OpenAI plans cover the agent surface.
  • SOAR playbooks are Logic Apps wired to Sentinel automation rules · auto-disable risky agent identities and page on-call.
  • Microsoft Security Copilot capacity is optional · sized per engagement budget.
Part III

Layer 2 — Service

Day 15. The platform has been quiet for two weeks while Priya validated the foundation — identities resolve, Key Vault returns secrets, the managed environment scales, Sentinel correlates events. Now it's time to land the first Service on top of it. The Service Bicep module she's about to deploy is much smaller than the platform module — by design. Most of what RC-E2E-03 needs already exists at Layer 1. What the Service module adds is its own slice: a Microsoft Fabric workspace, a Bronze landing for D365 and IoT, a Silver conformance Notebook, the Gold marts The Pricer will read, an MCP host Container App, the workflow substrate that triggers on excursion events.

A Service is a productized envelope that composes one or more agents over the APEX Platform: a KPI envelope (the commercial commitment), a scope (which agents and which MCP tools), a persona (who approves), an audit-row pattern (what lands in the LEDGER), and a two-contract commercial shape. RC-E2E-03 — the Pricing & Revenue Decision Service — is the running example in this guide because it's the canonical Magentic-pattern Service with all six envelopes fully exercised. The broader catalog of 38 unique service IDs across seven practices (Appendix E) follows the same Bicep template; differences are mostly in the parameters.

Three chapters cover the Service layer. Chapter 7 answers "what is a deployable Service" — the seven components every Service Bicep declares. Chapter 8 walks Service composition atop the Platform — how the Service module references Layer 1 outputs without duplicating them. Chapter 9 tackles multi-Service per tenant — how the second, third, and fourth Service add cleanly to the same Layer 1 without trampling each other. After Part III, Priya's tenant has one Service running end-to-end — but the agent containers it references are still placeholders. Layer 3 fills them in.

Chapters in this Part

  • Chapter 7What Is a Deployable Service
  • Chapter 8Service Composition Atop Platform
  • Chapter 9Multi-Service per Tenant
Chapter 7

What Is a Deployable Service

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Defines a Service as the unit of deployment, the unit of commercial envelope, and the unit of audit attribution.
  • Walks the RC-E2E-03 envelope as the canonical worked example.
  • Introduces the 38-service catalog and the seven practices that organize it.
60-second read
  • A Service is the unit of deployment · the unit of commercial envelope · the unit of audit attribution. 1:1:1 enforced.
  • RC-E2E-03 is the canonical example: 5 agents · 2 scenarios · KPI envelope GM +3.2pp / DoH −28% / Markdown-to-Clear +41% / $142K margin protected.
  • The catalog: 38 Services across 7 practices (RC · HLS · ER · AXLE · TH · TMT · ICE) — Appendix E enumerates all of them.
  • A Service Bicep deploys Microsoft.App/containerApps + Service-scoped data plane atop Layer 1; it never duplicates Layer 1 resources.

7.1 Three Definitions, One Service

"Service" is overloaded. In this book it means three things at once, and the deployment architecture must handle all three:

  1. The unit of deployment. A Bicep module set that consumes Layer 1 outputs and instantiates Microsoft.App/containerApps for each agent in the Service, plus Service-specific Microsoft.Storage / Microsoft.DocumentDB / Microsoft.Sql / Microsoft.Fabric data plane.
  2. The unit of commercial envelope. One Service has one KPI envelope, one persona model, one scope statement, one fee structure (W2 fixed-price or W3 outcome-based) — the elements documented in commercial/<service>-service-envelope.md for each cataloged Service.
  3. The unit of audit attribution. Every LEDGER row carries a service_code column. Audit decisions roll up to the Service that owned the agent that made the decision; KPI realization is computed per-Service.

If any one of those three drifts — for example, two distinct deployment modules sharing one commercial envelope, or one deployment module spanning two service codes — your audit attribution will not reconcile. Sprint 1 onwards enforces 1:1:1 between deployment, envelope, and service code.

7.2 The Canonical Example: RC-E2E-03

RC-E2E-03 — Pricing & Revenue Decision Service is the running example throughout the book. From MVP/docs/commercial/RC-E2E-03-service-envelope.md:

Envelope elementRC-E2E-03 value
Service codeRC-E2E-03
PracticeRetail & Consumer (RC)
DomainPricing & Revenue
TitlePricing & Revenue Decision Service — Cold-Chain & Markdown
Scenarios in scope (W2 pilot)Cold-chain excursion mid-shift · Dynamic markdown optimization
Agents composedThe Analyst · The Demand Checker · The Finance Lead · The Operations Lead · The Briefer
KPI envelopeGross Margin +3.2pp · Days-on-Hand −28% · Markdown-to-Clear +41% · per-event margin protected ~$142K (cold-chain) · decision-loop 5.7 hr → <90 sec
Personas (HITL)Marisol Reyes (Store Operations Lead) · Daniel Chen (Merchandising Director)
Commercial postureW2 fixed-price → W3 outcome-based with margin-share trigger
Substrate targetLab v0.1 (DEV) Q1 2026 → STAGE Q2 2026 → first client tenant Q3 2026

RC-E2E-03 is small enough to grasp in a single pass and large enough to exercise every layer: five agents (so Layer 3 is not trivial), two distinct scenarios (so the harness exercises branching), real-time + batch flows (so Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments uses both Consumption and Dedicated profiles), and a live KPI envelope (so the audit chain has something to attribute against).

7.3 The Catalog: 38 Services Across Seven Practices

RC-E2E-03 is one of 38 unique service IDs in the APEX catalog, sourced from APEX-Scenario-Chains.xlsx (Scenario Library sheet). Appendix E lists every Service code, practice, and domain. The seven practices:

PracticeCodeService-ID prefixes# ServicesExample Services
