Appendix B

Per-Tenant Onboarding Checklist (Day-Zero)

B.1 Client-Side Prerequisites

  1. ☐ Azure subscription provisioned under client EA · subscription ID recorded in Tenant Profile draft.
  2. ☐ Microsoft Defender for Cloud enabled at subscription scope.
  3. ☐ Microsoft Defender for Containers enabled · container registry registries auto-scan policy active.
  4. ☐ Microsoft Defender for Key Vault enabled · alerts routed to Microsoft Sentinel (if licensed) or to Azure Monitor action group.
  5. ☐ Microsoft Defender for App Service enabled · Container Apps coverage active.
  6. ☐ Deployment service principal created in client Microsoft Entra ID · federated credential preferred.
  7. ☐ Deployment service principal granted Contributor at the future resource group + custom role for Microsoft.KeyVault key write.
  8. ☐ Microsoft Cost Management tag inheritance configured · apex:* tags in inherited list.
  9. ☐ Microsoft Sentinel licensed? (Y/N — if Y, plan workspace-solution attach post-Layer-1)
  10. ☐ Microsoft Purview account identified for tenant catalog.

B.2 Independence & Language Audit

  1. ☐ Repository regex for "partner", "alliance", "strategic alliance" returns zero hits.
  2. ☐ Bicep parameter names contain none of: partner, alliance, joint.
  3. ☐ Tenant Profile description uses "Microsoft platform capabilities" / "Microsoft-native deployment" / "DMTSP".
  4. ☐ Statement of Work confirms two-contract structure; ECIF (if any) flows direct from Microsoft.

B.3 Bicep What-If & Deploy

  1. ☐ Tenant Profile parameter file authored (parameters/<tenant-code>-<suffix>.bicepparam).
  2. ☐ az deployment sub what-if review with client architect — all resources show + Create; no ~ Modify or - Delete.
  3. ☐ az deployment sub create executed; deployment succeeds in 12–18 minutes.
  4. ☐ All seven Microsoft resource types from §4.1 in Succeeded state.

B.4 Smoke Test

  1. ☐ Microsoft Defender for Containers shows the new container registry registered.
  2. ☐ Test image (agent-base:1.0.0) imports into client container registry; Microsoft Notation verify passes.
  3. ☐ No-op Container App revision deploys; /healthz returns 200.
  4. ☐ Application Insights receives a trace from the no-op revision.
  5. ☐ Microsoft.KeyVault crypto sign with CMK returns a valid signature.
  6. ☐ LEDGER chain seed row written; verify_chain returns ok=True.

B.5 Handoff

  1. ☐ Tenant Profile YAML committed to delivery repo.
  2. ☐ Tenant registered in central Service catalog.
  3. ☐ Microsoft Sentinel solution attached to Layer 1 workspace (if licensed).
  4. ☐ Microsoft Purview registers Layer 1 assets.
  5. ☐ On-call rotation includes the new tenant.
Appendix C

Per-Service Onboarding Checklist

Run this checklist for any of the 38 Services from Appendix E being onboarded into a tenant whose Layer 1 is live.

C.1 Pre-Deploy

  1. ☐ Tenant Profile resolved; Layer 1 version satisfies Service's platform_compatible range.
  2. ☐ Service envelope file (commercial/<service-code>-service-envelope.md) reviewed and current.
  3. ☐ Compatibility matrix (COMPATIBILITY.md) confirms agent versions compatible with Service version.
  4. ☐ Agent image SHA digests recorded in Service parameter file.

C.2 Image Mirror

  1. ☐ For each agent in Service composition: az acr import from Deloitte authoring registry to client tenant registry.
  2. ☐ Microsoft Notation signatures + cosign attestations verified post-import.
  3. ☐ Microsoft Defender for Containers re-scan completes clean.

C.3 Service-Scoped Secrets

  1. ☐ <service-code>-ledger-dsn written to Microsoft.KeyVault.
  2. ☐ <service-code>-severity-weights (or equivalent runner config) written.
  3. ☐ <service-code>-mcp-tools-config written.
  4. ☐ Per-secret access policy: runtime / MCP identities only.

C.4 Deploy

  1. ☐ az deployment group what-if against tenant resource group · review with Service owner.
  2. ☐ az deployment group create · all Microsoft.App/containerApps + per-Service data plane succeed.
  3. ☐ Tags include apex:service-code and apex:service-version.

C.5 Validate

  1. ☐ Test harness runs full UC catalog for Service · all UCs PASS.
  2. ☐ LEDGER chain verifies for the harness run.
  3. ☐ Application Insights shows traces for every agent.
  4. ☐ Microsoft Defender for Containers runtime telemetry shows no anomalies during harness run.

C.6 Promote

  1. ☐ SOR connections live (PROD only) · bronze landing receives data.
  2. ☐ HITL personas notified · Teams adaptive cards routing confirmed.
  3. ☐ KPI baseline captured · attribution rolling.
  4. ☐ Service marked active in tenant Service catalog.
Appendix D

Agent Promotion Pipeline

The six-stage Microsoft.ContainerRegistry pipeline from §10.2, expanded with sample command sequences and gate evidence.

D.1 Pipeline Stages

StageMicrosoft tool / APIGate evidence
Buildaz acr build (Microsoft.ContainerRegistry tasks)Build log · resulting image digest
ScanMicrosoft Defender for Containers (auto on push)Assessment ID · severity counts
Sign (Notation)notation sign with key in Microsoft.KeyVault HSMNotation signature artifact
Sign (cosign)cosign sign with key in Microsoft.KeyVaultCosign signature + attestation
Verifynotation verify against authoring trust policyVerification log
Mirroraz acr import per client tenantPer-tenant import log
Pull-time verifyMicrosoft.App/containerApps revision pull · notation verify hookContainer App revision health

D.2 Promotion Cadence

An agent promotion follows three windows:

  • Continuous — every merge to main runs Build + Scan + Sign in the Deloitte authoring registry. The image is available but not yet promoted.
  • Daily — at end of day, eligible images (passed scan, passed Notation verify) are mirrored to Lab v0.1 client container registry and become canary-eligible.
  • Per upgrade window — on the documented upgrade cadence (Chapter 15), images mirror to STAGE/PROD client container registries.

D.3 Attestation Predicate

Cosign attestations carry the in-toto predicate apex.deloitte/agent-attestation with these fields (compact form):

JSON — attestation predicate
{
  "predicateType": "apex.deloitte/agent-attestation/v1",
  "predicate": {
    "image_digest": "sha256:6a4c...",
    "agent_id":     "the-analyst",
    "agent_version": "1.4.0",
    "git_commit":   "abc123...",
    "platform_compatible": ">=0.1.0,<0.3.0",
    "scan_assessment_id": "/subscriptions/.../assessments/...",
    "scan_summary": { "high": 0, "medium": 0, "low": 3 },
    "build_timestamp": "2026-05-09T14:23:11Z",
    "build_task_id": "apex-acr-task-the-analyst-build-1842"
  }
}

Appendix G

Glossary

Azure Container Registry · Microsoft.ContainerRegistry/registries — Microsoft's managed OCI registry. Premium SKU is mandatory for APEX (geo-replication, content trust, CMK encryption, zone redundancy). Microsoft's CLI uses the abbreviation ACR (e.g., az acr build), but in Microsoft commercial contexts ACR also means Azure Consumption Revenue; this book spells the registry term out in prose to avoid the collision. CLI commands, the apex-acr.azurecr.io hostname, the acr.bicep module filename, and the AcrPull RBAC role name retain Microsoft's literal naming.

Application Insights · Microsoft.Insights/components — Workspace-based APM that emits to Microsoft.OperationalInsights. Connection string auto-injected into Microsoft.App/containerApps via the managed environment's daprAIConnectionString.

Azure Container Apps · Microsoft.App/containerApps — Managed serverless container runtime. Each agent in a Service is one Container App.

Azure Logic Apps — Microsoft platform workflow runtime. APEX uses Logic Apps Hybrid (Logic Apps Standard runtime hosted on Microsoft.App/containerApps with kind=workflowapp) as the default Azure trigger + workflow substrate — receiving events, calling the agent fleet over a single HTTP POST, and routing downstream responses. Multi-agent coordination lives inside Microsoft Agent Framework, not Logic Apps (per Services Guide §14.4 and §14B.13). Logic Apps Standard on Microsoft.Web/sites and Microsoft.Logic/workflows Consumption remain available for niche trigger cases. Substrate counterpart of n8n.

Bicep — The Microsoft platform IaC language. All APEX Platform and Service deployments are Bicep-defined.

CMK — Customer-Managed Key. APEX CMK is RSA-HSM 2048 in Microsoft.KeyVault Premium, providing FIPS 140-2 Level 3 root of trust for container registry encryption, Key Vault soft-delete recovery, and LEDGER checkpoint signing.

DAG Orchestrator — Used in earlier APEX-M drafts to describe a substrate-side multi-agent dispatcher. Deprecated since Microsoft Agent Framework GA. The substrate (n8n on laptop · Logic Apps Hybrid on Microsoft platform) is now a trigger + HTTP entry point; multi-agent coordination (Sequential · Concurrent · Handoff · Group Chat · Magentic) is composed inside the agent fleet by Microsoft Agent Framework's per-pattern builder classes. See Services Guide §14.4 / §14B.13.

Container Apps Environment · Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments — Layer 1 resource hosting all Container Apps in a tenant. Binds Log Analytics + Application Insights; declares workload profiles (Consumption, Dedicated D4).

Cosign — OCI image signing tool. Used alongside Microsoft Notation for cross-tool ecosystem compatibility.

DMTSP — Deloitte Microsoft Technology & Services Practice. The Independence-compliant phrasing for the Deloitte–Microsoft delivery relationship.

ECIF — Microsoft Enterprise Customer Investment Funds. Flows direct from Microsoft to client; never through Deloitte.

HITL — Human-in-the-loop. Operator approval gate within an agent decision flow.

HSM · Microsoft Hardware Security Module — Microsoft.KeyVault Premium-backed HSM module. RSA-HSM keys never leave the HSM in cleartext.

Independence — The audit-relationship rule constraining Deloitte–Microsoft co-delivery. Enforced architecturally in APEX via two contracts, two container registries, separated subscriptions, and disciplined language.

KEDA — Kubernetes Event-Driven Autoscaling. Built into Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments runtime; rules declared on Microsoft.App/containerApps.

KQL — Kusto Query Language. The query language for Microsoft.OperationalInsights and Application Insights.

LEDGER — APEX append-only audit table. Hash-chained (_hash, _prev_hash); checkpoint signing with the Microsoft.KeyVault HSM CMK.

Logic Apps Hybrid — covers two hosting fabrics per Microsoft Learn. APEX-M defaults to Logic Apps Standard on Container Apps (Microsoft.App/containerApps · kind=workflowapp) because it amortizes onto the same Layer 1 Container Apps Environment as the agents. The alternative — Logic Apps Hybrid on Azure Arc-enabled Kubernetes (Microsoft.Web/kubeEnvironments with a SQL Server runtime state store) — is selected only for engagements with an existing Arc-enabled K8s footprint (per Ch 2.6.2).

MCP — Model Context Protocol. The agent's tool-call interface. APEX MCP tool catalog is scoped per Service via the mi-apex-merch-mcp identity.

Microsoft Defender for Cloud / for Containers / for Key Vault / for App Service — Microsoft's cloud security posture management. APEX requires Defender for Containers for image scanning + runtime threat detection.

Microsoft Entra ID — Microsoft's identity provider (formerly Microsoft Entra ID). All APEX runtime authentication is via Microsoft Entra ID-issued user-assigned managed identity tokens.

Microsoft Fabric · Microsoft.Fabric/capacities — Unified analytics platform. Lakehouses host Gold serving for APEX Services.

Microsoft Notation — Microsoft's content-trust signing tool. Strict trust policies enforce signed-only image deployment.

Microsoft Purview · Microsoft.Purview/accounts — Catalog and lineage. APEX assets register with the tenant Purview account for audit-and-discoverability.

Microsoft Sentinel — Microsoft's SIEM/SOAR. Attached as a workspace solution on Layer 1's Log Analytics workspace; UEBA on the four user-assigned managed identities.

n8n — Open-source workflow automation. APEX uses self-hosted n8n in docker-compose as the laptop-tier orchestration substrate, preserving the §0.5 zero-cost dev guarantee. Substrate counterpart of Azure Logic Apps. Does not deploy to client tenants.

Notation trust policy — JSON document declaring registry scopes, trust stores, and verification level. APEX uses level "strict."

SemVer — Semantic versioning. Independent SemVer per layer: apex-platform, per-Service, per-agent.

Service — In this book, one of the 38 productized envelopes from Appendix E. The unit of deployment, commercial envelope, and audit attribution.

Substrate — The runtime environment for agents. APEX recognizes four substrate tiers: laptop (Docker Desktop), DEV (Lab v0.1 Azure), STAGE, PROD.

Tenant Profile — YAML artifact emitted at Day-Zero recording the Microsoft platform resource IDs and identifiers for a client tenant. Consumed by every subsequent Service onboarding.

Two-contract model — Independence-compliant commercial structure: client contracts Microsoft for platform, client contracts Deloitte separately for services. ECIF flows direct.

UC · Use Case — Test harness scenario. Each UC drives a multi-step exercise of a Service through deployed Microsoft.App/containerApps endpoints.

W1 · W2 · W3 — APEX Wave structure. W1 Foundation (4–8 weeks); W2 Pilot (6–15 months, Lab v0.1 → STAGE → first PROD tenant); W3 Scale & Fuse (multi-year, fortieth-tenant scale-out and cross-Service composition).

WORM — Write-once, read-many. The append-only enforcement applied to LEDGER tables in Microsoft.Sql or Microsoft.DBforPostgreSQL.

Workflow parity — The contract that every orchestration in APEX is maintained as a single conceptual flow with substrate-specific JSON for both n8n (laptop) and Azure Logic Apps (Microsoft platform). Repository layout: workflow.intent.md + workflow.n8n.json + workflow.logicapp.json. CI verifies the two stay in sync.

Workload profile — Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments capacity option. APEX uses Consumption (default, scale-to-zero) and Dedicated D4 (steady-state, higher concurrency).

Zero-cost development — The §0.5 design goal: the developer's inner loop runs on a laptop with no Azure consumption. Achieved by free, self-hostable substitutes for every Azure component (Postgres for Azure Database for PostgreSQL, n8n for Azure Logic Apps, docker-compose for Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments, .env for Microsoft.KeyVault, stdout for Azure Monitor, stub for Microsoft Entra ID). The first cost line is the DEV (Lab v0.1) substrate transition.

Closing Independence Reminder

Throughout this guide and the deployment artifacts derived from it, the Deloitte–Microsoft relationship is described as "Deloitte's Microsoft practice," "DMTSP," "Microsoft platform capabilities," or "Microsoft-native deployment." Never partner, alliance, or strategic alliance. The two-contract commercial structure — client ↔ Microsoft for platform, client ↔ Deloitte for services, ECIF direct — is enforced architecturally by the boundaries described in Chapters 1, 3, 10, and 13. End of book.

Appendix H

PowerShell Deployment Cookbook — Line-Level Commands for Every Motion

This appendix is the PowerShell counterpart to the az CLI examples that appear inline throughout the book. Where the body chapters illustrate a motion, this appendix gives you the exact PowerShell commands to run it. Use it as a copy-paste cookbook for Day-Zero Layer 1 deployment, per-Service Layer 2 deployment, agent image mirroring, smoke testing, and rollback — all driving Bicep against the Microsoft platform.

Note — When to choose PowerShell over Azure CLI

The Azure CLI (az) and Az PowerShell module are functionally equivalent for Bicep deployments — both call the same Microsoft.Resources REST API. PowerShell is the natural choice when you are already in a PowerShell shell on Windows, when you want to pipeline objects between Microsoft.Resources cmdlets, or when you need to script in a way that integrates with other Az PowerShell modules (Microsoft.Graph, AzureAD, Microsoft.PowerBI). Both tools accept identical Bicep parameter files; switching shells does not change Bicep behavior.

H.1 Prerequisites

H.1.1 Install the Az PowerShell module

Run once per workstation. The Az meta-module pulls every Microsoft platform sub-module needed for Bicep deployment, Microsoft.KeyVault, Microsoft.ContainerRegistry, Microsoft.App, and Microsoft Entra ID.

POWERSHELL — install Az module
# Confirm PowerShell version (Az module requires 7.4+ or Windows PowerShell 5.1)
$PSVersionTable.PSVersion

# Install Az for current user (no admin required); allow prerelease for newer Bicep features
Install-Module -Name Az -Repository PSGallery -Scope CurrentUser -AllowClobber -Force

# Verify Bicep CLI is reachable from PowerShell (Az.Resources downloads it on first deploy)
Get-AzContext  # triggers Az module load
bicep --version

H.1.2 Pin the Bicep version your team uses

Bicep is shipped separately from the Az module. Pin the version to keep deployments reproducible across delivery leads.

POWERSHELL — pin Bicep version
# Install / upgrade Bicep CLI through the Az module's helper
Install-AzBicep -Version "0.30.23"   # match the version recorded in your delivery repo

# Verify
Get-AzBicepVersion

H.2 Authentication into the Client Tenant

Three authentication modes appear in deployment work. Choose by where you are running.

H.2.1 Interactive (developer workstation)

POWERSHELL — interactive sign-in
Connect-AzAccount -TenantId $env:CLIENT_TENANT_ID

# After sign-in, point the session at the client subscription
Set-AzContext -Subscription $env:CLIENT_SUBSCRIPTION_ID

# Confirm
(Get-AzContext).Subscription | Format-List Name, Id, TenantId

H.2.2 Federated service principal (CI / pipeline)

The Independence-preferred mode — no client secret on disk.

POWERSHELL — federated SP sign-in
# In a pipeline, $env:AZURE_FEDERATED_TOKEN is provided by the CI runtime (GitHub Actions OIDC,
# Azure DevOps workload identity federation, etc.)
Connect-AzAccount `
    -ServicePrincipal `
    -TenantId      $env:CLIENT_TENANT_ID `
    -ApplicationId $env:DEPLOY_SP_APP_ID `
    -FederatedToken $env:AZURE_FEDERATED_TOKEN

Set-AzContext -Subscription $env:CLIENT_SUBSCRIPTION_ID

H.2.3 Service principal with certificate (legacy fallback)

POWERSHELL — certificate SP sign-in
Connect-AzAccount `
    -ServicePrincipal `
    -TenantId         $env:CLIENT_TENANT_ID `
    -ApplicationId    $env:DEPLOY_SP_APP_ID `
    -CertificateThumbprint $env:DEPLOY_SP_CERT_THUMBPRINT
Warning — Never commit client secrets

If your authentication path requires -CertificatePath or -Credential with a password, store the credential in Microsoft.KeyVault and resolve at runtime via Get-AzKeyVaultSecret. Plain-text secrets in PowerShell scripts have a way of ending up in audit logs.

H.3 Day-Zero Layer 1 Deployment

This is the PowerShell counterpart to Chapter 13's az deployment sub what-if and az deployment sub create. It deploys MVP/agentic-merch/bicep/main.bicep against the client subscription.

H.3.1 Set deployment-time variables

POWERSHELL — Layer 1 variables
$ClientTenantCode    = 'contoso-na'
$DeploymentSuffix    = 'lab01'
$Location            = 'centralus'
$ResourceGroupName   = "rg-apex-merch-$DeploymentSuffix"
$DeploymentName      = "apex-platform-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmm')"

$BicepRoot           = 'C:\Stage\Clients\Industries\Consumer\Merch\MVP\agentic-merch\bicep'
$TemplateFile        = Join-Path $BicepRoot 'main.bicep'
$ParametersFile      = Join-Path $BicepRoot "parameters\$ClientTenantCode-$DeploymentSuffix.bicepparam"

H.3.2 Dry run — Get-AzSubscriptionDeploymentWhatIfResult

Before any resource is created, walk the what-if output line by line with the client architect. Every entry should be + Create; any ~ Modify or - Delete halts the deploy.

POWERSHELL — what-if
$whatIf = Get-AzSubscriptionDeploymentWhatIfResult `
    -Location          $Location `
    -TemplateFile      $TemplateFile `
    -TemplateParameterFile $ParametersFile `
    -ResultFormat      FullResourcePayloads

# Render the change list
$whatIf.Changes | Format-Table ChangeType, ResourceId

# Save full diff for the architect review
$whatIf | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 10 |
    Out-File -Encoding utf8 "C:\tmp\layer1-whatif-$DeploymentName.json"

H.3.3 Deploy — New-AzSubscriptionDeployment

POWERSHELL — Layer 1 deploy
$deployment = New-AzSubscriptionDeployment `
    -Name                  $DeploymentName `
    -Location              $Location `
    -TemplateFile          $TemplateFile `
    -TemplateParameterFile $ParametersFile `
    -Verbose

# Inspect the deployment object — succeeded, failed, in progress
$deployment | Format-List ProvisioningState, Timestamp, CorrelationId

# Pull the Tenant Profile outputs for handoff
$deployment.Outputs.GetEnumerator() | ForEach-Object {
    [PSCustomObject]@{
        Name  = $_.Key
        Value = $_.Value.Value
    }
} | Format-Table -AutoSize

H.3.4 Wait for completion when running async

For long deployments (Premium ACR provisioning is the slowest single step at ~6 minutes), use -AsJob and monitor:

POWERSHELL — async + monitor
$job = New-AzSubscriptionDeployment `
    -Name                  $DeploymentName `
    -Location              $Location `
    -TemplateFile          $TemplateFile `
    -TemplateParameterFile $ParametersFile `
    -AsJob

# Poll deployment state
do {
    Start-Sleep -Seconds 30
    $state = (Get-AzSubscriptionDeployment -Name $DeploymentName).ProvisioningState
    Write-Host "[$(Get-Date -Format 'HH:mm:ss')] state=$state"
} until ($state -in 'Succeeded','Failed','Canceled')

if ($state -ne 'Succeeded') {
    Get-AzSubscriptionDeploymentOperation -DeploymentName $DeploymentName |
        Where-Object { $_.ProvisioningState -eq 'Failed' } |
        Format-List TargetResource, StatusMessage
    throw "Deployment $DeploymentName ended in state $state"
}

H.3.5 Persist the Tenant Profile

Convert the deployment outputs into the Tenant Profile YAML described in §13.6.

POWERSHELL — emit Tenant Profile
$o = $deployment.Outputs

$tenantProfile = @"
tenant_code: $ClientTenantCode
client_tenant_id: $((Get-AzContext).Tenant.Id)
client_subscription_id: $((Get-AzContext).Subscription.Id)
deployment_suffix: $DeploymentSuffix
location: $Location
platform_version: 0.1.0
acr_login_server: $($o.acrLoginServer.Value)
keyvault_uri: $($o.keyvaultUri.Value)
log_analytics_workspace_id: $($o.logAnalyticsWorkspaceId.Value)
container_apps_env_id: $($o.containerAppsEnvId.Value)
managed_identities:
  runtime: $($o.runtimeIdentityId.Value)
  mcp:     $($o.mcpIdentityId.Value)
"@

$tenantProfile | Out-File -Encoding utf8 `
    "C:\Stage\Clients\Industries\Consumer\Merch\MVP\delivery\tenant-profiles\$ClientTenantCode-$DeploymentSuffix.yaml"

H.4 Per-Service Layer 2 Deployment

Once the Tenant Profile exists, every Service onboarding into the same tenant is a resource-group-scope deployment of the Service's Bicep module.

H.4.1 Load the Tenant Profile and Service variables

POWERSHELL — Layer 2 variables
$ServiceCode    = 'rc-e2e-03'
$ServiceVersion = '1.0.0'
$ServiceBicep   = Join-Path $BicepRoot "services\$ServiceCode\service.bicep"
$ServiceParams  = Join-Path $BicepRoot "services\$ServiceCode\$ServiceCode-$DeploymentSuffix.bicepparam"
$ServiceDeploymentName = "$ServiceCode-$ServiceVersion-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmm')"

# Pull Tenant Profile values from the Layer 1 deployment outputs (or YAML)
$tp = Get-AzSubscriptionDeployment -Name $DeploymentName

# Compose Service-level parameters (override agent SHA digests dynamically)
$serviceParameters = @{
    serviceCode        = $ServiceCode
    serviceVersion     = $ServiceVersion
    deploymentSuffix   = $DeploymentSuffix
    containerAppsEnvId = $tp.Outputs.containerAppsEnvId.Value
    runtimeIdentityId  = $tp.Outputs.runtimeIdentityId.Value
    mcpIdentityId      = $tp.Outputs.mcpIdentityId.Value
    acrLoginServer     = $tp.Outputs.acrLoginServer.Value
    keyvaultUri        = $tp.Outputs.keyvaultUri.Value
    appInsightsConnectionString = $tp.Outputs.appInsightsConnectionString.Value
}

H.4.2 Deploy the Service

POWERSHELL — Layer 2 deploy
# Resource group already exists from Layer 1 deploy
$serviceDeployment = New-AzResourceGroupDeployment `
    -Name                $ServiceDeploymentName `
    -ResourceGroupName   $ResourceGroupName `
    -TemplateFile        $ServiceBicep `
    -TemplateParameterObject $serviceParameters `
    -Verbose

$serviceDeployment | Format-List ProvisioningState, Timestamp

H.4.3 What-if at resource-group scope

POWERSHELL — RG-scope what-if
Get-AzResourceGroupDeploymentWhatIfResult `
    -ResourceGroupName       $ResourceGroupName `
    -TemplateFile            $ServiceBicep `
    -TemplateParameterObject $serviceParameters |
    Select-Object -ExpandProperty Changes |
    Format-Table ChangeType, ResourceId

H.5 Container Registry Operations — Mirror Agent Images

The PowerShell counterpart to az acr import is Import-AzContainerRegistryImage. This is the action that crosses the Independence boundary from the Deloitte authoring registry to the client tenant registry.

POWERSHELL — image mirror
$ClientRegistryName    = "apexacr$DeploymentSuffix"
$AuthoringRegistryFqdn = 'apex-acr.azurecr.io'
$AgentImage            = 'agent-the-analyst'
$AgentDigest           = 'sha256:6a4c3e2b81d9f4a2c5e7b9d1a3f8c6e2d4b7a9f1c8e5b3d7a2f4c9e6b1d8a3f7'

# Mirror by digest — bytes + signatures + cosign attestations
Import-AzContainerRegistryImage `
    -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName `
    -RegistryName      $ClientRegistryName `
    -SourceRegistryUri $AuthoringRegistryFqdn `
    -SourceImage       "$AgentImage@$AgentDigest" `
    -TargetTag         "$AgentImage@$AgentDigest"

# Confirm the digest landed
Get-AzContainerRegistryManifest `
    -RegistryName  $ClientRegistryName `
    -RepositoryName $AgentImage |
    Where-Object { $_.Digest -eq $AgentDigest } |
    Format-List Digest, Tags, ImageSize, LastUpdateTime
Independence — Mirror is the only crossing

Import-AzContainerRegistryImage is the single PowerShell call that crosses tenant boundaries. Audit logs in both Microsoft.ContainerRegistry instances record the import; pair it with the Microsoft Notation verify in §H.6 to make the chain reconstructible.

H.6 Microsoft Notation Verify in PowerShell

The notation CLI is invoked from PowerShell as a native external command. Wrap it for clean exit-code handling.

POWERSHELL — verify signature
$imageRef = "$ClientRegistryName.azurecr.io/$AgentImage@$AgentDigest"

# notation verify exits non-zero on policy mismatch — capture and surface
$verifyOutput = & notation verify $imageRef 2>&1
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Notation verify FAILED for $imageRef`n$verifyOutput"
}
Write-Host "✓ Notation verify passed for $imageRef" -ForegroundColor Green

H.7 Microsoft.KeyVault Operations

H.7.1 Write a Service-scoped secret

POWERSHELL — write secret
$VaultName = "apexkv$DeploymentSuffix"
$SecretName = "$ServiceCode-ledger-dsn"
$SecretValue = ConvertTo-SecureString `
    "Server=tcp:apex-sql-$DeploymentSuffix.database.windows.net;Database=apex_ledger;Authentication=Active Directory Managed Identity;" `
    -AsPlainText -Force

Set-AzKeyVaultSecret `
    -VaultName   $VaultName `
    -Name        $SecretName `
    -SecretValue $SecretValue `
    -ContentType 'application/x-postgres-dsn'

H.7.2 Sign a LEDGER checkpoint with the HSM CMK

POWERSHELL — HSM sign
$KeyName       = 'apex-merch-cmk'
$CheckpointBytes = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes($checkpointHash)

$signature = Invoke-AzKeyVaultKeyOperation `
    -Operation Sign `
    -Algorithm RS256 `
    -VaultName $VaultName `
    -Name      $KeyName `
    -ByteArrayValue $CheckpointBytes

[Convert]::ToBase64String($signature.RawResult)  # store on the LEDGER row

H.7.3 Recover a soft-deleted secret

POWERSHELL — recover from soft-delete
Get-AzKeyVaultSecret -VaultName $VaultName -InRemovedState |
    Format-Table Name, DeletedDate, ScheduledPurgeDate

Undo-AzKeyVaultSecretRemoval -VaultName $VaultName -Name $SecretName

H.8 Container Apps Revisions — Canary & Traffic Split

Microsoft.App/containerApps revision multi-mode powers the canary motion in Chapter 15. Az PowerShell exposes it via the Az.App module.

H.8.1 Update an agent to a new digest in canary

POWERSHELL — canary deploy
$AppName = "app-$ServiceCode-the-analyst-$DeploymentSuffix"
$NewDigest = 'sha256:9c1d4e7a2b5f8c3d6e9b2a4f7c1d3e6b8a5f2c9d4e7b1a8f3c6d2e5b9a4f7c1d'
$NewImage = "$ClientRegistryName.azurecr.io/agent-the-analyst@$NewDigest"

# Switch the app to multi-revision mode (idempotent)
Update-AzContainerApp `
    -ResourceGroupName    $ResourceGroupName `
    -Name                 $AppName `
    -ConfigurationActiveRevisionsMode 'Multiple'

# Deploy the new revision (alongside current)
Update-AzContainerApp `
    -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName `
    -Name              $AppName `
    -TemplateContainer @(@{
        Name  = 'the-analyst'
        Image = $NewImage
    })

# Set traffic split: 95% current, 5% new revision
$current = (Get-AzContainerAppRevision -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName -ContainerAppName $AppName |
            Where-Object { $_.Active -eq $true } |
            Sort-Object CreatedTime -Descending)

$traffic = @(
    @{ RevisionName = $current[1].Name; Weight = 95 }
    @{ RevisionName = $current[0].Name; Weight = 5  }
)

Update-AzContainerApp `
    -ResourceGroupName    $ResourceGroupName `
    -Name                 $AppName `
    -ConfigurationIngressTraffic $traffic

H.8.2 Promote — ramp 5 → 25 → 50 → 100

POWERSHELL — canary ramp
function Set-CanarySplit {
    param([int]$NewWeight)
    $traffic = @(
        @{ RevisionName = $current[1].Name; Weight = (100 - $NewWeight) }
        @{ RevisionName = $current[0].Name; Weight = $NewWeight }
    )
    Update-AzContainerApp `
        -ResourceGroupName    $ResourceGroupName `
        -Name                 $AppName `
        -ConfigurationIngressTraffic $traffic
    Write-Host "[$(Get-Date -Format 'HH:mm:ss')] traffic split: $((100 - $NewWeight))/$NewWeight"
}

Set-CanarySplit -NewWeight 25
# observe Microsoft.Insights for ~30 min before next ramp
Set-CanarySplit -NewWeight 50
Set-CanarySplit -NewWeight 100

H.8.3 Snap rollback to prior revision

POWERSHELL — snap rollback
$traffic = @( @{ RevisionName = $current[1].Name; Weight = 100 } )

Update-AzContainerApp `
    -ResourceGroupName    $ResourceGroupName `
    -Name                 $AppName `
    -ConfigurationIngressTraffic $traffic

Write-Host "✓ Rolled back to revision $($current[1].Name)" -ForegroundColor Green

H.9 Smoke Test in PowerShell

The smoke test from §13.5 against deployed Microsoft.App/containerApps endpoints.

POWERSHELL — smoke test
$App = Get-AzContainerApp -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName -Name $AppName
$AgentFqdn = $App.ConfigurationIngressFqdn

# Healthz
$health = Invoke-RestMethod -Uri "https://$AgentFqdn/healthz" -Method GET
$health | Format-List status, agent_id, version

# Run a canonical fixture via /run
$body = @{
    request_id   = [guid]::NewGuid().ToString()
    service_code = $ServiceCode.ToUpper()
    scenario     = 'cold-chain-excursion'
    inputs       = @{
        store_id     = 'BIGBOX-100'
        excursion_minutes = 47
    }
} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5

$response = Invoke-RestMethod `
    -Uri    "https://$AgentFqdn/run" `
    -Method POST `
    -ContentType 'application/json' `
    -Body   $body

$response | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6

H.10 Validate the LEDGER Hash Chain

POWERSHELL — chain verify
$harnessFqdn = (Get-AzContainerApp -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName -Name "harness-$DeploymentSuffix").ConfigurationIngressFqdn

$verify = Invoke-RestMethod `
    -Uri    "https://$harnessFqdn/ledger/verify?run_id=$RunId" `
    -Method GET

if ($verify.ok -ne $true) {
    throw "LEDGER chain broken at row $($verify.broken_at): $($verify.message)"
}
Write-Host "✓ Chain verified · rows=$($verify.rows_checked)" -ForegroundColor Green

H.11 Rollback Motion in PowerShell

The PowerShell counterpart to Chapter 16's rollback story. Three layers, three reversal paths.

H.11.1 Layer 3 rollback (seconds)

See §H.8.3 — traffic-split snap to prior revision.

H.11.2 Layer 2 rollback (~10 min)

POWERSHELL — Service redeploy from prior version
# Reset to prior Service Bicep parameter file from git
git -C "$BicepRoot\..\.." checkout HEAD~1 -- "agentic-merch/bicep/services/$ServiceCode/$ServiceCode-$DeploymentSuffix.bicepparam"

# Redeploy Service
$rollbackName = "$ServiceCode-rollback-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmm')"
New-AzResourceGroupDeployment `
    -Name                  $rollbackName `
    -ResourceGroupName     $ResourceGroupName `
    -TemplateFile          $ServiceBicep `
    -TemplateParameterFile $ServiceParams `
    -Verbose

H.11.3 Layer 1 rollback (30+ min)

POWERSHELL — Platform redeploy of prior version
# Roll the Bicep templates back to the prior Layer 1 tag
git -C "$BicepRoot\..\.." checkout apex-platform/v0.1.0 -- agentic-merch/bicep/

# Redeploy Layer 1
$rollbackName = "apex-platform-rollback-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmm')"
New-AzSubscriptionDeployment `
    -Name                  $rollbackName `
    -Location              $Location `
    -TemplateFile          $TemplateFile `
    -TemplateParameterFile $ParametersFile `
    -Verbose

# Microsoft.KeyVault soft-delete restore (if a key was lost)
Get-AzKeyVault -VaultName $VaultName -InRemovedState
Undo-AzKeyVaultRemoval -VaultName $VaultName -Location $Location -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName

H.12 One-Liners Cheat Sheet

POWERSHELL — useful one-liners
# List all APEX-tagged resources in the tenant
Get-AzResource -Tag @{ 'apex:layer' = 'platform' } | Format-Table Name, ResourceType, Location

# Find every Container App for a given Service
Get-AzContainerApp -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName |
    Where-Object { $_.Tag.'apex:service-code' -eq 'RC-E2E-03' } |
    Format-Table Name, ConfigurationIngressFqdn, LatestRevisionName

# Pull last hour of agent traces from Application Insights
$ai = Get-AzApplicationInsights -ResourceGroupName $ResourceGroupName -Name "apex-ai-$DeploymentSuffix"
$query = @"
customEvents
| where name == 'ledger.row.committed'
| where timestamp > ago(1h)
| project timestamp, agent=tostring(customDimensions.agent_id), service=tostring(customDimensions.service_code)
"@
Invoke-AzOperationalInsightsQuery -WorkspaceId $tp.Outputs.logAnalyticsWorkspaceId.Value -Query $query

# Aggregate Microsoft Cost Management by service-code tag
Get-AzConsumptionUsageDetail `
    -BillingPeriodName (Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMM') `
    -Expand 'meterDetails' |
    Where-Object { $_.Tags.'apex:service-code' -eq 'RC-E2E-03' } |
    Measure-Object -Property PretaxCost -Sum |
    Select-Object Sum

# Compile a Bicep file to ARM JSON for review
bicep build $TemplateFile  # emits main.json next to main.bicep

# Lint a Bicep file before commit
bicep lint $TemplateFile

H.13 PowerShell ↔ Azure CLI Equivalents

Quick translation table for delivery leads who switch shells.

MotionAzure CLI (bash)Az PowerShell
Sign in (interactive)az loginConnect-AzAccount
Set subscriptionaz account set -s <id>Set-AzContext -Subscription <id>
What-if (sub scope)az deployment sub what-ifGet-AzSubscriptionDeploymentWhatIfResult
Deploy (sub scope)az deployment sub createNew-AzSubscriptionDeployment
Deploy (RG scope)az deployment group createNew-AzResourceGroupDeployment
Mirror imageaz acr importImport-AzContainerRegistryImage
Build imageaz acr buildNew-AzContainerRegistryBuild *
List Container Appsaz containerapp listGet-AzContainerApp
Update Container App revisionaz containerapp updateUpdate-AzContainerApp
Set traffic splitaz containerapp ingress traffic setUpdate-AzContainerApp -ConfigurationIngressTraffic
Read Key Vault secretaz keyvault secret showGet-AzKeyVaultSecret
Sign with HSM keyaz keyvault key signInvoke-AzKeyVaultKeyOperation -Operation Sign
Run KQL queryaz monitor log-analytics queryInvoke-AzOperationalInsightsQuery

* In some Az module versions, New-AzContainerRegistryBuild is exposed only via the Az.ContainerRegistry preview module; check Get-Module -ListAvailable Az.ContainerRegistry and pin a known-good version.

Best Practice — Keep both shells fluent

Az PowerShell and Azure CLI are not in competition; they are two surfaces over the same Microsoft.Resources REST API. Most delivery leads use whichever shell they happen to be sitting in. Pin the same Bicep version across both, and your deployments behave identically.

Appendix J

APEX-M Deployment Glossary

Cross-reference: comprehensive APEX-M term glossary in Services Guide Appendix M. This appendix collects the deployment-specific terms — Bicep resources · API versions · deployment-script names · operational concepts — that recur across this volume.

TermDefinition
azdAzure Developer CLI · canonical APEX-M deploy tool · azd provision + azd deploy covers most flows
AVMAzure Verified Module · Microsoft-curated Bicep modules with WAF-aligned defaults (e.g., avm/res/fabric/capacity)
Bicep what-ifPre-deploy preview · mandatory pre-merge per §19.6 engineering standards
cosign attestationContainer image signature anchor · gate for STAGE → PROD promotion · paired with Microsoft Notation signature
CPDCompliance Posture Document · per-engagement reference to in-scope frameworks · signed by both compliance owners (Services Appendix L · Ch 19.7)
deploymentScriptsMicrosoft.Resources/deploymentScripts · Bicep resource that runs PowerShell or AzCLI · APEX-M uses for Fabric · Purview · Entra config not yet Bicep-native
Eval gatedeploymentScripts invocation of Foundry batch evaluation · blocks promotion if any evaluator below threshold (per §11.6.3)
F SKUMicrosoft Fabric capacity SKU class — F2, F8, F32, F64, F128, F256, F512, F1024, F2048; APEX-M PROD entry is typically F64 or F128
Federated identity credentialOIDC trust between Microsoft.ManagedIdentity and external IdP (GitHub Actions · Foundry blueprint) · eliminates client secrets
Image SHA promotionPromote the same image bytes Lab → DEV → STAGE → PROD by digest reference · no per-stage rebuild · the bytes that passed STAGE eval are the bytes that run in PROD
OIDC federationCI/CD authenticates to Azure via OIDC trust · no static secrets in pipeline variables
Paired regionMicrosoft-defined region pair (e.g., East US 2 + West US 3) · used by Azure services for geo-replication · target for APEX-M DR failover
PIMPrivileged Identity Management · JIT role activation · APEX-M uses for capacity admin, KV admin, subscription owner
SCUSecurity Compute Unit · Microsoft Security Copilot capacity unit · 1 SCU is the Lab/DEV minimum, 4 SCU typical PROD entry
UAMIUser-Assigned Managed Identity · Microsoft.ManagedIdentity/userAssignedIdentities · APEX-M's four standard UAMIs are runtime · mcp · ingest · pii-unlock
WORMWrite-Once-Read-Many · storage immutability mode used for LEDGER blob retention

Appendix L

End-to-End Deployment Runbook

The deployment-team companion to Sellers Guide §38 (Pursuit Walkthrough) and Services Guide Appendix N (Migration / Brownfield + 90-day Service rollout). This appendix sequences the technical work for a new APEX-M engagement from kickoff through go-live, with explicit gates and named artifacts.

L.1 Day 0 — Engagement Kickoff

  • Client tenant identified · subscription created/identified · Microsoft Entra ID landing-zone confirmed
  • Engagement-lead break-glass identity provisioned · PIM-eligible
  • Region pair selected (Microsoft-paired regions; CPD documents data residency)
  • GitHub / Azure DevOps repo initialised from APEX-M template repo · CODEOWNERS configured
  • Lab v0.1 provisioned on engagement-team laptops (Docker Desktop · zero Azure cost) — verifies team can run the dev loop

L.2 Days 1–14 — Layer 1 Platform Provisioning

  • Bicep blueprint scoped · platform parameter set authored
  • azd provision in DEV — creates resource group · UAMIs · KV Premium · ContainerRegistry · Log Analytics workspace · Application Insights component
  • Microsoft.Fabric DEV capacity F8 provisioned · per-Service workspace via deployment-scripts/fabric-workspaces.ps1
  • Microsoft.Purview DEV account provisioned · per-practice domain created
  • Microsoft.CognitiveServices ContentSafety account provisioned
  • Foundry resource + DEV project provisioned · base model deployments created
  • Sentinel onboarded · seven detection rules deployed in report-only · SOAR playbooks deployed

L.3 Days 15–30 — Layer 2 First Service

  • Pick the lead Service (typically RC-E2E-03 in RC engagements; HLS-E2E-04 in HLS)
  • Bronze ingestion via Microsoft Fabric Mirroring from primary SOR
  • Silver conformance Notebook · canonical schema family applied
  • Gold mart authored (one decision shape · one Lakehouse-or-Warehouse) · semantic model in Power BI Direct Lake
  • MCP host Container App deployed · per-Service MCP tools registered · row-hash registry seeded
  • Eventstream + Eventhouse + Activator wired (for trigger-driven Services)

L.4 Days 31–45 — Layer 3 Agent Fleet

  • Five-agent fleet authored (Analyst · Specialist · Demand Checker · Finance Lead · Operations Lead · Briefer); use case YAML completed
  • Loader binds per §14.5.8 · agent images built · cosigned · pushed
  • Foundry agent applications created · published · per-agent identities issued · Bicep role assignments applied
  • n8n laptop wiring tested · Logic Apps Hybrid deployed for DEV cloud equivalence
  • Microsoft Teams adaptive-card HITL flow piloted with engagement-team personas
  • Three-gate evaluation framework wired · golden set authored (50–500 cases)

L.5 Days 46–60 — STAGE Promotion

  • STAGE Bicep blueprint deployed · Microsoft.Fabric F32 capacity
  • Private endpoints enabled on all Microsoft platform resources
  • Conditional Access flipped to enabled · agent-id CA policies validated
  • DLP + Insider Risk Management deployed in monitor mode (one-week observation)
  • Eval gate passes at threshold 0.85 over golden set
  • Microsoft Notation signature applied to STAGE image set
  • Compliance Posture Document signed by both compliance owners
  • Sentinel detections flipped from report-only to enabled

L.6 Days 61–90 — PROD Cutover

  • PROD Bicep blueprint deployed · F64 or F128 per practice
  • Full private-endpoint topology · public access disabled
  • DLP + IRM flipped to enforce
  • DSPM for AI active · sensitivity-label inheritance enabled
  • Image SHA promoted by digest from STAGE registry (no rebuild)
  • Cosign attestation verified · CPD signed for PROD
  • Day-2 runbook signed · on-call rotation populated (24×7 follow-the-sun)
  • Wave 1 KPI baseline captured · cost-per-decision dashboard live
  • Microsoft Security Copilot capacity provisioned (if engagement-licensed)
  • Chaos Studio experiment scheduled in STAGE for quarterly cadence
  • Sustain motion handover complete · managed-services partner accepts

L.7 Day 90+ — Wave 2 Planning

  • Service #2 in Wave 1 begins L.3 (often parallel with Service #1's L.6)
  • Wave 2 Service candidates scoped · Cost envelope updated per practice
  • Cross-Service composition planned (where one Service consumes another's Gold via shortcut)
  • Capacity SKU upgrade decision (F128 → F256 if per-practice demand justifies)
  • Quarterly model audit · agent retrain cycle initiated where eval drift surfaces
Chapter M365

Surfacing APEX-M in Microsoft 365 + Power Platform

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Documents the path to surface APEX-M agents inside Microsoft 365 Copilot, Microsoft Teams, and Microsoft Copilot Studio.
  • Maps APEX-M Foundry Hosted Agents to Microsoft 365 Custom Engine Agents via the Microsoft 365 Agents Toolkit.
  • Locates Power Platform integration points (Power Automate flows, Dataverse organizational context, Power BI dashboards).
60-second read
  • APEX-M's Foundry Hosted Agent + Microsoft 365 Agents Toolkit = Custom Engine Agent for M365 Copilot — the same hosted agent surfaces in M365 Copilot Chat, Teams personal scope, and Outlook actions.
  • For lighter-touch read-from-knowledge agents, use Copilot Studio Declarative Agents; APEX-M agents stay on Foundry but expose MCP endpoints Copilot Studio consumes.
  • Power Automate is the natural orchestration substrate for non-agent workflow steps (HITL approval routing, ticket creation, Teams Adaptive Card publication). Dataverse is the M365-native organizational-context source.

M365.1 The four publishing paths

Microsoft's 2026 release wave 1 documents four canonical paths for getting an enterprise agent in front of users. APEX-M agents fit the right-most column (pro-code, hosted on Foundry):

FeatureCopilot StudioTeams AIM365 Agents SDKFoundry (APEX-M)
ApproachLow-codePro-codePro-codePro-code
ToolingCopilot Studio UITeams SDK + VS CodeM365 Agents ToolkitM365 Agents Toolkit + Foundry portal
ChannelsM365 Copilot, Teams, partner apps, mobileM365 Copilot, TeamsM365 Copilot, Teams, partner apps, mobileM365 Copilot, Teams (other channels via custom integration)
OrchestratorCopilot StudioTeams Action PlannerBring your own (Semantic Kernel, LangChain, Agent Framework)Foundry workflows + Microsoft Agent Framework / LangGraph
ModelsCopilot StudioAnyAnyFoundry model catalog (gpt-4o, Llama, DeepSeek, …)
APEX-M fitRead-from-knowledge agents (declarative)——Default for every featured-scenario agent

M365.2 Foundry Hosted Agent → M365 Custom Engine Agent

The default APEX-M publishing path:

  1. Agent code (Microsoft Agent Framework or LangGraph) is built and deployed via azd deploy per Deployment Guide §11.5 (Layer 3 Surfacing via Foundry Agent Service).
  2. Microsoft 365 Agents Toolkit packages the agent as a Custom Engine Agent manifest. The manifest references the Foundry-hosted endpoint and the agent's tool surface.
  3. Deploy the package to the M365 admin center via Integrated Apps. Admins approve; the agent appears in M365 Copilot Chat + Teams personal scope.
  4. Per-engagement: optionally submit to the Microsoft Commercial Marketplace for multi-tenant agents Deloitte ships across clients.

The advantage of Custom Engine over Declarative agents: full control over orchestration, models, deterministic execution, multi-step workflow, HITL gates, and APEX-Core protocol composition. APEX-M's six-agent fleet per scenario relies on this model — declarative agents can't do the multi-agent orchestration the cold-chain or markdown scenarios require.

M365.3 When to use a Declarative agent instead

For specific use cases where the agent only retrieves and answers questions over a knowledge source (e.g., "what is RC-E2E-03's KPI envelope?"), a Copilot Studio Declarative Agent is the right shape:

  • Lower setup cost — no Foundry project required for the lookup path
  • Native M365 Copilot orchestration (no APEX runtime intervention)
  • Automatic SharePoint / OneDrive grounding with sensitivity-label inheritance per Purview for Copilot Studio

APEX-M can publish a Declarative agent for the read-only lookup path while the Foundry-hosted Custom Engine Agent handles decision-making. Typical pattern: a single service code has both — RC-E2E-03's "what's our pricing policy?" Declarative agent and RC-E2E-03's "approve this markdown" Custom Engine Agent.

M365.4 Power Platform integration

Three Power Platform integrations every APEX-M tenant should plan for:

M365.4.1 Power Automate — non-agent workflow steps

Power Automate is the natural Microsoft-native target for parts of an APEX-M scenario that aren't agent decisions: HITL approval routing through Teams Adaptive Cards, ticket creation in ServiceNow, email notifications, scheduled refresh. Per the Power Platform 2026 wave 1 release plan, Power Automate now supports Copilot Studio-powered actions in cloud flows — APEX-M agents can call these actions as MCP tools.

M365.4.2 Dataverse — organizational-context source

Dataverse provides M365-native organizational context (people, Teams messages, Exchange inboxes, Loop pages) that APEX-M agents can read via MCP. Per Microsoft's 2026 wave 1 release plan, Dataverse exposes a native MCP server and a Python SDK; APEX-M's MCP composition layer (per book §10) consumes them as ordinary tools. Dataverse is also a clean fit for client-portal data models — Power Pages on Dataverse, with APEX-M agents reading Dataverse rows as they would Silver entities.

M365.4.3 Power BI — KPI surfacing

Per Services Guide §1.4, Gold marts are exposed via Power BI Direct Lake semantic models. APEX-M dashboards use the native Direct Lake connection — no data movement, no scheduled refresh. The merchandising director's weekly markdown review (RC-E2E-03 Daniel Chen persona) lives in Power BI; agents publish proposed markdowns to a Power BI report tile via the same Direct Lake model.

M365.5 Independence + Microsoft 365 surfacing

APEX-M's surfacing in Microsoft 365 + Power Platform is clean from an Independence perspective: the client already holds M365 + Power Platform licensing; APEX-M is integrating with the client's existing investment. Microsoft 365 Agents Toolkit is open-source tooling; Custom Engine Agent packaging is an industry-standard manifest pattern. Per APEX-Core Independence Posture §Language standards, APEX-M deliverables describe these integrations as honoring the client's existing Microsoft 365 / Power Platform investment — never as Deloitte–Microsoft co-marketing.

Key Takeaways

  • Default APEX-M publishing: Foundry Hosted Agent → M365 Agents Toolkit → Custom Engine Agent → M365 Copilot + Teams.
  • Use Copilot Studio Declarative agents for read-only knowledge lookups; same service can have both shapes.
  • Power Automate handles non-agent workflow; Dataverse provides M365-native context via MCP; Power BI Direct Lake surfaces Gold marts.
  • Surfacing in M365 honors the client's existing investment — no Independence concern.
Chapter A

Composing Client-Approved Adapters with APEX-M

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Shows how an APEX-M deployment honors a client's mixed-cloud or SaaS-heavy architecture without abandoning the Microsoft primary.
  • Walks the use-case client_approved_architecture block end-to-end against APEX-Core protocols.
  • Maps the 8 priority adapters (cloud / SaaS / SIEM / identity) to APEX-Core protocol slots and shows which to pick when.
60-second read
  • APEX-M is the primary variant — it owns where agents run, where identity is rooted, and which IaC dialect (Bicep) deploys the platform.
  • Adapters integrate everything else the client's CAB has approved — AWS RDS for POS data, Snowflake on AWS for analytics, Splunk for SIEM, Okta for federated identity.
  • Adapter bindings live in the use-case YAML's client_approved_architecture block. The wizard validates every adapter ref resolves and that each adapter satisfies the protocol the slot expects.
  • SEC Independence: every adapter has a sec_independence.md stating Deloitte does not resell, sublicense, or have an alliance posture with the integrated provider.

A.1 The shape — primary variant + composed adapters

A typical client APEX-M deployment looks like this in the wild:

┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  APEX-M (primary)                                          │
│   · Foundry Agent Service                                  │
│   · Microsoft Entra Agent ID                               │
│   · Microsoft Fabric (OneLake / Eventhouse)                │
│   · Microsoft Purview Audit (system of record)             │
│   · Microsoft Defender for AI                              │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            │ composes
            ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Client-approved adapters (per CAB)                        │
│   · cloud.aws.rds       → Mirror POS + ERP from AWS        │
│   · cloud.gcp.bigquery  → Read marketing analytics on GCP  │
│   · saas.salesforce     → CRM as Silver source             │
│   · saas.snowflake      → Snowflake-on-AWS Gold consumer   │
│   · siem.splunk         → Parallel-write to client Splunk  │
│   · identity.okta       → Federated IdP                    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

The APEX-M primary owns the Microsoft layer. The adapters honor the rest of the client's existing investment without forcing migration.

A.2 Use case YAML — client_approved_architecture

Each use case under services/{ind}/{code}/use-cases/{slug}/use-case.yaml declares its adapter bindings:

YAML — example for contoso-rc-e2e-03-na-pilot
primary_variant: APEX-M

client_approved_architecture:
  data_lake:
    primary: { variant: APEX-M, target: fabric.onelake.rc-canonical }
    bronze_sources:
      - { source: pos, adapter: cloud.aws.rds, dataset: contoso-pos-prod }
      - { source: erp, adapter: cloud.azure.adls_gen2, dataset: sap-s4-mirror }
  identity:
    primary: { variant: APEX-M, source: entra-agent-id }
    federation: [ identity.okta ]
  siem_audit:
    primary: { variant: APEX-M, target: purview-audit }
    secondary: [ siem.splunk ]
  crm:
    adapter: saas.salesforce
    org: contoso-prod
  hitl_channel:
    primary: { variant: APEX-M, target: teams }
    fallback: [ collaboration.slack ]

The wizard's validator walks this block and confirms every adapter: reference resolves to a directory under packages/apex-adapters/src/apex_adapters/protocol_adapters/, and that the adapter's SATISFIES list includes the APEX-Core protocol the slot expects.

A.3 Picking adapters — the priority eight

The Phase 0 stub catalog covers eight common client patterns. Implementations build per-engagement when the client's CAB has approved the integration.

AdapterSatisfiesWhen to pick it
cloud.aws.s3DataLake (Bronze)Client has POS / IoT / batch data landing in S3 — mirror to APEX-M Fabric Bronze
cloud.aws.rdsDataLake (Mirroring source)Client runs RDS for the SOR (POS, ERP, CRM) — Fabric Mirroring brings it Silver-ready
cloud.gcp.bigqueryDataLake (Silver/Gold)Client has marketing or analytics workloads on BigQuery — read as Silver
cloud.gcp.pubsubMessageBusClient publishes events from GCP — route into APEX-M Eventstream
saas.salesforceDataLake (CRM)Salesforce is the CRM SOR — RC-E2E-04 Loyalty + HLS Patient relationships
saas.snowflakeDataLake (Gold consumer)Client's BI analysts run on Snowflake — feed APEX-M Gold into client Snowflake
siem.splunkAuditLedger (secondary) + ObservabilityClient mandates Splunk SIEM — parallel-write while Purview Audit stays primary
identity.oktaAgentIdentityProvider (federation)Client workforce sits in Okta — federate to Microsoft Entra Agent ID

A.4 SEC Independence per adapter

Every adapter has a sec_independence.md with Deloitte's verbatim Independence statement. The pre-publish CI lane (per Roadmap.md BL.P.194) blocks any APEX deliverable using disallowed terminology — "preferred cloud," "alliance," "partnership" — across all variants and adapters.

Best Practice — One adapter per CAB approval

Do not pre-deploy adapters "for future use." An adapter is only ever active when the client's Cloud Architecture Board has formally approved the integrated provider. The use-case YAML records the CAB approval in its commit history; auditors trace from client_approved_architecture back to the approval document.

A.5 Cross-references

  • APEX-Core Independence Posture — the variant equality + language standards story
  • APEX-Core Adapter Catalog — full inventory of adapters + how to add one
  • APEX-Core Protocols Reference — what each adapter slot expects
  • APEX-Core Variant Comparison — when to switch primary variant entirely versus composing adapters
  • services/_use-case.schema.md — the full use-case YAML schema

Key Takeaways

  • APEX-M is the primary variant; adapters compose every non-Microsoft service the client's CAB has approved.
  • Adapter bindings live in the use-case YAML's client_approved_architecture block; the wizard validates every reference.
  • Eight priority adapters (cloud × 4, SaaS × 2, SIEM × 1, identity × 1) ship as stubs in Phase 0; concrete implementations build per-engagement.
  • SEC Independence: every adapter has its own sec_independence.md; APEX does not have an alliance posture with any cloud or SaaS provider.
Appendix M · New 2026-05

Fabric IQ Deployment Mechanics

Tenant settings · region affinity · F-SKU floor · Git + Fabric deployment pipelines · Operations Agent provisioning.

Currency pin · May 2026

When the client adopts Fabric IQ, the substrate-side mechanics this volume describes need six additions. Existing Day-Zero, per-Service, image-mirror, and rollback runbooks remain correct — Fabric IQ items deploy alongside them. Read this addendum after Chapter 13 (Day-Zero) and Chapter 14 (Pre-deployment Security Gate).

M.1 The six deployment-side notes

D1 · F2+ capacity floor; no trial capacities for Operations Agent. Per Microsoft Learn, Fabric Data Agents require "a paid F2 or higher Fabric capacity, or a Power BI Premium per capacity (P1 or higher) capacity with Microsoft Fabric enabled." Operations Agent is stricter still: "Trial capacities aren't supported." Laptop and dev substrates that previously ran on Fabric Trial must either upgrade to F2+ or use a Foundry agent stand-in for Operations-Agent-led workloads. Add to Appendix B (Per-Tenant Onboarding) as gate item B.NN.

D2 · Cross-region capacity affinity required for Data Agent. Direct quote: "The Fabric data agent can't execute queries when the data source's workspace capacity is in a different region than the data agent's workspace capacity. For example, a lakehouse with capacity in North Europe fails if the Data Agent's capacity is in France Central." Multi-region tenants need a region-affinity matrix in the deployment plan: which Data Agent lives in which region, which workspaces share that region. Add a new check to Appendix C (Per-Service Onboarding): "C.NN · Region affinity validation."

D3 · Cross-geo processing/storage tenant setting. Per Microsoft Learn, Fabric Data Agents require enabling "cross-geo processing and cross-geo storing for AI" in tenant settings if the Fabric capacity isn't provisioned in US or EU regions. Operations Agent has the same prerequisite. Add to Appendix B as item B.NN with cross-reference to the Power BI admin portal screenshot in the operations-agent doc.

D4 · Workspace outbound access protection → PSG-16 candidate. Direct quote: "Fabric data agents operate within workspace outbound access protection boundaries. Workspace administrators can manage permitted outbound connections through the workspace settings to control which external endpoints the data agent can reach." Proposed new gate PSG-16 (Workspace Outbound Boundary) — evaluated at deploy time, verifies the workspace's outbound allow-list permits only the Foundry endpoint, the LEDGER endpoint, and the Purview ingest endpoint. Wire into Chapter 14 (Pre-deployment Security Gate) alongside PSG-1..15.

D5 · Fabric deployment pipelines + Git integration for Data Agent assets. Per Microsoft Learn, Fabric Data Agents support three ALM capabilities natively:

  • Diagnostics — built-in, surfaces query generation issues and selects-data-source decisions
  • Git integration — workspace ↔ Git for version-controlling agent configurations (instructions, example queries, data source selections)
  • Deployment pipelines — Fabric-native dev → test → prod promotion

The APEX deploy.yml workflow layers on top of these mechanics rather than replacing them. Sequence at promotion time:

  1. GitHub Action triggers (PR merge or tag)
  2. APEX wizard runs PSG-1..16 against target substrate
  3. Bicep what-if for Foundry / Container Apps / Storage resources
  4. Fabric deployment pipeline promotes Data Agent + Ontology + Operations Agent items from source workspace to target workspace
  5. Wizard records the audit row tying both the Bicep deployment ID and the Fabric pipeline deployment ID

Add the workflow snippet to Appendix D (Agent Promotion Pipeline) as a new sequence variant: "D.NN · Fabric IQ promotion path."

D6 · Operations Agent Teams app provisioning. Per Microsoft Learn: "To enable the agent to contact you proactively when it identifies data that matches the defined rules, install the Fabric Operations Agent Teams app." Add to Appendix B: "B.NN · Install Fabric Operations Agent Teams app in target tenant. Confirm recipients have write permissions on the agent item." Recipients without write permission cannot receive proactive messages.

M.2 Bicep status

The Fabric IQ items are workspace-level Fabric resources, not first-class Azure resources. As of May 2026:

ResourceBicep statusNote
Microsoft.Fabric/capacitiesGA · API 2023-11-01Carries F-SKU; required for Fabric IQ workloads
WorkspaceFabric admin API · not pure BicepBicep deployment script wraps the workspace-create REST call
Data Agent itemFabric REST API · not BicepProvisioned through Fabric portal or REST; promoted via Fabric deployment pipelines (see D5)
Ontology itemFabric REST API (preview) · not BicepSame as Data Agent
Operations Agent itemFabric REST API (preview) · not BicepSame as Data Agent
Activator (eventstream-fed)Fabric REST API · not BicepUsed by Operations Agent for action execution

The APEX-managed Bicep modules in Appendix A stay focused on the Azure plane (Container Apps, Container Registry, Key Vault, Application Insights, Identity, Networking). Fabric IQ items deploy through the Fabric control plane — APEX's responsibility is the orchestration wrapper (PSG gates, audit row, Fabric pipeline trigger).

M.3 Identity model · creator-bound vs. workload-bound

Direct quote from the Operations Agent doc: "The agent operates by using the delegated identity and permissions of its creator. When a recipient approves a recommendation, the agent executes the action on behalf of the creator, using the creator's permissions."

Implication for deployment: Operations Agents are creator-bound, not workload-identity-bound. This differs from Foundry-hosted agents (which run under a managed identity) and from custom MCP servers (which run under a workload identity). Day-Zero provisioning sequence:

  1. Create a dedicated Fabric Operations Agent creator service account in Entra (not a managed identity — a real account)
  2. Grant that account the required permissions to all data sources, Activator items, and Power Automate flows the agent will need
  3. Create all Operations Agent items under that service account
  4. Document the creator account in Appendix B as a required Day-Zero artefact
  5. Audit: every Operations Agent action is attributed to the creator account, not to the approving recipient

Without a dedicated creator account, Operations Agents inherit the permissions of whichever individual created them — which breaks under personnel changes. The dedicated creator account is the Fabric IQ analogue of a workload identity.

New Virtual Views shipped with CFMP v0.2

CFMP contributes 6 new Virtual Views spanning MERML (merchandise), CXML (customer experience), and the new cfmp namespace. All are declarative manifests; the runtime federates them through the standard apex-orchestrator data path.

Virtual View IDPurpose
merml.product_with_planogram_locationPer-SKU shelf location joined from MERML.Product × CFMP.StoreMap. Refreshed nightly from the planogram feed; drives the agent's route_to_product tool.

  • Proposed Interface #15 — Maps & Wayfinding · APEX-M: Azure Maps Creator (Indoor Maps + Wayfinding REST + Web SDK). APEX-G: Google Maps Geospatial Creator. APEX-A: Amazon Location Service indoor maps. Industry Packs consume the abstract interface; the cloud-profile YAML resolves the implementation.
